### AIR CONDITIONING UNIT > REMOVAL

AIR CONDITIONING UNIT > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE BATTERY NEGATIVE TERMINAL
Wait for 90 seconds after disconnecting the cable to prevent the airbag working.
2. REMOVE ENGINE UNDER COVER NO.1 (w/ Under Cover and Intercooler)
3. DRAIN ENGINE COOLANT (w/ Intercooler)
4. REMOVE RADIATOR GRILLE (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 2 clips and 2 screws.
Release the 6 claws and 4 clips and remove the radiator grille.
5. REMOVE AIR INLET DUCT (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 3 clips and the air inlet duct No.2.
Remove the 2 bolts, 2 clips and the air inlet duct No.1.
6. DRAIN WINDSHIELD WASHER FLUID (w/ Intercooler)
7. REMOVE WINDSHIELD WASHER JAR ASSEMBLY (w/o Rear Wiper and w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 2 bolts.
Separate the hose and connector, and remove the windshield washer jar assembly.
8. REMOVE WINDSHIELD WASHER JAR ASSEMBLY (w/ Rear Wiper and Intercooler)
Remove the 2 bolts.
Separate the 2 hoses and 2 connectors, and remove the windshield washer jar assembly.
9. REMOVE RADIATOR RESERVE TANK ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the bolt, and separate the radiator reserve tank assembly from the body panel.
Remove the clip, and disconnect the water by-pass hose No.1.
Remove the clip, and disconnect the water by-pass hose No.2.
Remove the radiator reserve tank assembly.
10. REMOVE HOOD LOCK ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 3 bolts, hood lock control cable and the hood lock assembly.
11. REMOVE RADIATOR SUPPORT SUB-ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 6 bolts, 2 screws and the radiator upper support.
12. REMOVE INTERCOOLER ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Loosen the clamp, and disconnect the air hose No.2.
Loosen the clamp, and disconnect the air hose No.3.
Remove the 3 bolts and the intercooler assembly.
13. DISCONNECT COOLER REFRIGERANT SUCTION PIPE A
Remove the bolt, and slide the hook connector.
Disconnect the cooler refrigerant suction pipe A.
Remove the O-ring from the cooler refrigerant suction pipe A.
NOTICE:
Seal the openings of the disconnected parts using vinyl tape to prevent moisture and foreign matter from entering.
14. DISCONNECT COOLER REFRIGERANT LIQUID PIPE B
Disconnect the cooler refrigerant liquid pipe B.
Remove the O-ring from the cooler refrigerant liquid pipe B.
NOTICE:
Seal the openings of the disconnected parts using vinyl tape to prevent moisture and foreign matter from entering.
15. DISCONNECT HEATER WATER HOSE OUTLET
Using pliers, grip the claws of the clip and slide the clip to disconnect the heater outlet water hose.
16. DISCONNECT HEATER WATER HOSE INLET A
HINT:
Disconnection of the heater water outlet hose A is the same as for the heater water inlet hose A.
17. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL ASSEMBLY
HINT:
Refer to the instruction for removal of the instrument panel assembly. (
Click here
)
18. REMOVE STEERING INTERMEDIATE SHAFT
Remove the 3 bolts and the steering shaft cover.
Remove the bolt.
Slide the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2 and put matchmarks on the steering gear assembly and the steering intermediate shaft assembly No.2.
19. REMOVE STEERING COLUMN ASSEMBLY
Disconnect the connectors and wire harness clamps from the steering column assembly.
Remove the 3 bolts and the steering column assembly.
20. REMOVE AIR DUCT NO.1
Remove the clip.
Release the 2 claws and remove the air duct No.1.
21. DISCONNECT PARKING BRAKE CONTROL HANDLE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts and disconnect the parking brake control handle sub-assembly.
22. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL TO FLOOR BRACE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 5 bolts and the instrument panel to floor brace sub-assembly.
23. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL REINFORCEMENT ASSEMBLY
Remove the clamps and connectors, and then disconnect the wire harness.
Remove the bolts, nuts and junction blocks.
Remove the 2 caps and 2 bolts.
Remove the 2 bolts and nut. (AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSION)
Remove the 3bolts and nut. (MANUAL TRANSMISSION)
Using a"Torx" socket wrench (T40), remove the 4 "Torx" bolts.
HINT:
The "Torx" bolts on the passenger side can be removed with the collar for adjustment.
Using a hexagon wrench 12 mm, remove the 2 collars and instrument panel reinforcement assembly.
24. REMOVE AIR CONDITIONER UNIT ASSEMBLY
Remove the 3 bolts and air conditioner unit assembly.
25. REMOVE AIR DUCT NO.2
Release the 2 claws and remove the air duct No.2.
26. REMOVE AIR CONDITIONING RADIATOR ASSEMBLY
w/ PTC
Remove the screw and then disconnect the wire harness.
Remove the 3 screws and air conditioning radiator assembly.

7. DRAIN WINDSHIELD WASHER FLUID (w/ Intercooler)
8. REMOVE WINDSHIELD WASHER JAR ASSEMBLY (w/o Rear Wiper and w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 2 bolts.
Separate the hose and connector, and remove the windshield washer jar assembly.
9. REMOVE WINDSHIELD WASHER JAR ASSEMBLY (w/ Rear Wiper and Intercooler)
Remove the 2 bolts.
Separate the 2 hoses and 2 connectors, and remove the windshield washer jar assembly.
10. REMOVE RADIATOR RESERVE TANK ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the bolt, and separate the radiator reserve tank assembly from the body panel.
Remove the clip, and disconnect the water by-pass hose No.1.
Remove the clip, and disconnect the water by-pass hose No.2.
Remove the radiator reserve tank assembly.
11. REMOVE HOOD LOCK ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 3 bolts, hood lock control cable and the hood lock assembly.
12. REMOVE RADIATOR SUPPORT SUB-ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Remove the 6 bolts, 2 screws and the radiator upper support.
13. REMOVE INTERCOOLER ASSEMBLY (w/ Intercooler)
Loosen the clamp, and disconnect the air hose No.2.
Loosen the clamp, and disconnect the air hose No.3.
Remove the 3 bolts and the intercooler assembly.
14. DISCONNECT COOLER REFRIGERANT SUCTION PIPE A
Remove the bolt, and slide the hook connector.
Disconnect the cooler refrigerant suction pipe A.
Remove the O-ring from the cooler refrigerant suction pipe A.
NOTICE:
Seal the openings of the disconnected parts using vinyl tape to prevent moisture and foreign matter from entering.
15. DISCONNECT COOLER REFRIGERANT LIQUID PIPE B
Disconnect the cooler refrigerant liquid pipe B.
Remove the O-ring from the cooler refrigerant liquid pipe B.
NOTICE:
Seal the openings of the disconnected parts using vinyl tape to prevent moisture and foreign matter from entering.
16. DISCONNECT HEATER WATER HOSE OUTLET
Using pliers, grip the claws of the clip and slide the clip to disconnect the heater outlet water hose.
17. DISCONNECT HEATER WATER HOSE INLET A
HINT:
Disconnection of the heater water outlet hose A is the same as for the heater water inlet hose A.
18. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL ASSEMBLY
HINT:
Refer to the instruction for removal of the instrument panel assembly. (
Click here
)
19. REMOVE STEERING INTERMEDIATE SHAFT
Remove the steering intermediate shaft (
Click here
).
20. REMOVE STEERING COLUMN ASSEMBLY
Remove the steering column assembly (
Click here
).
21. REMOVE AIR DUCT NO.1
Remove the clip.
Release the 2 claws and remove the air duct No.1.
22. DISCONNECT PARKING BRAKE CONTROL HANDLE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts and disconnect the parking brake control handle sub-assembly.
23. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL TO FLOOR BRACE SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 5 bolts and the instrument panel to floor brace sub-assembly.
24. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL REINFORCEMENT ASSEMBLY
Remove the clamps and connectors, and then disconnect the wire harness.
Remove the bolts, nuts and junction blocks.
Remove the 2 caps and 2 bolts.
Remove the 2 bolts and nut. (AUTOMATIC TRANSMISSION)
Remove the 3bolts and nut. (MANUAL TRANSMISSION)
Using a "Torx" socket wrench (T40), remove the 4 "Torx" bolts.
HINT:
The "Torx" bolts on the passenger side can be removed with the collar for adjustment.
Using a hexagon wrench 12 mm, remove the 2 collars and instrument panel reinforcement assembly.
25. REMOVE AIR CONDITIONER UNIT ASSEMBLY
Remove the 3 bolts and air conditioner unit assembly.
26. REMOVE AIR DUCT NO.2
Release the 2 claws and remove the air duct No.2.
27. REMOVE AIR CONDITIONING RADIATOR ASSEMBLY
w/ PTC
Remove the screw and then disconnect the wire harness.
Remove the 3 screws and air conditioning radiator assembly.

### AIR CONDITIONING UNIT > DISASSEMBLY

AIR CONDITIONING UNIT > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE MODE CONTROL CABLE
Using a screwdriver wrapped with protective tape, release the claw and remove the mode control cable.
2. REMOVE AIR MIX CONTROL CABLE
Using a screwdriver wrapped with protective tape, release the claw and remove the air mix control cable.
3. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL REGS CONNECTOR ASSEMBLY CENTER
Release the 4 claws and remove the instrument panel regs connector assembly center.
4. REMOVE QUICK HEATER ASSEMBLY (w/ PTC)
Remove the 2 screws and quick heater assembly.
5. REMOVE HEATER RADIATOR UNIT SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the screw and heater radiator unit sub-assembly.
6. REMOVE AIR DUCT NO.3
Remove the 2 screws.
Release the 2 claws and remove the air duct No.3.
7. REMOVE DRAIN COOLER HOSE
Remove the drain cooler hose.
8. REMOVE HEATER BRACKET
Remove the screw and heater bracket.
9. REMOVE COOLER EXPANSION VALVE
Using a hexagon wrench 4 mm, remove the 2 hexagon bolts and cooler expansion valve.
10. REMOVE COOLER EVAPORATOR SUB-ASSEMBLY NO.1
Remove the 4 screws.
Release the 2 claws and remove the heater case lower.
Release the clamp and remove the cooler evaporator sub-assembly.
11. REMOVE EVAPORATOR TEMPERATURE SENSOR
Remove the evaporator temperature sensor.

---

### AIR CONDITIONING UNIT > REASSEMBLY

AIR CONDITIONING UNIT > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL EVAPORATOR TEMPERATURE SENSOR
Install the sensor to the evaporator as shown in the illustration.
Check that the sensor sticks to the evaporator surface as shown in the illustration (A: Sensor, B: Evaporator).
NOTICE:
If reusing the evaporator, do not insert the sensor to the position that it had been inserted before. Insert it between position C shown in the illustration.
2. INSTALL COOLER EVAPORATOR SUB-ASSEMBLY NO.1
Sufficiently apply compressor oil (ND-OIL 8) to 2 new O-rings and the fitting surface. Install the 2 O-rings to the cooler evaporator No.1.
Compressor oil:
ND-OIL 8 or equivalent
Install the cooler evaporator sub-assembly.
Install the connector.
Install the heater case lower with the 2 claws and 4 screws.
3. INSTALL COOLER EXPANSION VALVE
Using a hexagon wrench 4 mm, install the cooler expansion valve with the 2 hexagon bolts.
Torque:
3.5 N*m{
35 kgf*cm
, 30 in.*lbf
}
4. INSTALL HEATER BRACKET
Install the heater bracket with the bolt.
5. INSTALL DRAIN COOLER HOSE
Install the drain cooler hose.
6. INSTALL AIR DUCT NO.3
Engage the 2 claws to install the air duct No.3.
Install the 2 screws.
7. INSTALL HEATER RADIATOR UNIT SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install the heater radiator unit sub-assembly with the screw.
8. INSTALL QUICK HEATER ASSEMBLY (w/ PTC)
Install the quick heater assembly with the 2 screws.
9. INSTALL INSTRUMENT PANEL REGS CONNECTOR ASSEMBLY CENTER
Engage the 4 claws to install instrument panel regs connector assembly.
10. INSTALL AIR MIX CONTROL CABLE
Fully turn the air mix control cable gear and unit gear in the direction indicated by the arrows to set them at "MAX. COOL".
Install the air mix control cable.
11. INSTALL MODE CONTROL CABLE
Fully turn the air mode control cable gear and unit link in the direction indicated by the arrows to set them at "FACE".
Install the mode control cable.

### FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS > INSTALLATION

FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. CLEAN FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS
Using a scraper, remove the spacers, moulding and adhesive from the back door glass.
Clean the outer edge of the back door glass with non-residue solvent.
NOTICE:
Do not touch the back door glass surface after cleaning it.
Even if using new back door glass, clean the back door glass with non-residue solvent.
2. CLEAN VEHICLE BODY
Clean and shape the contact surface of the vehicle body.
On the contact surface of the vehicle body, use a knife to cut away excess adhesive as shown in the illustration.
HINT:
Leave as much adhesive on the vehicle body as possible.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the vehicle body.
3. INSTALL FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS STOPPER
Apply Primer G to the glass where the spacers will be installed.
HINT:
If primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Install 2 new spacers onto the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
4. INSTALL FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS
Using a brush, apply Primer M to the exposed part of the vehicle body.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer M to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Do not apply Primer M to the adhesive.
Throw away any leftover Primer M.
Do not apply too much Primer M.
Using a brush or sponge, apply Primer G to the contact surface of the glass.
HINT:
If Primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Apply adhesive to the glass.
Adhesive:
Toyota Genuine Windshield Glass Adhesive or equivalent
Cut off the tip of cartridge nozzle as shown in the illustration.
Specification:
Area
Measurement
a
12.0 mm (0.472 in.)
b
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
HINT:
After cutting off the tip, use all adhesive within the time written in the table below.
Usage timeframe
Temperature
Usage Timeframe
35°C (95°F)
15 minutes
20°C (68°F)
1 hour 40 minutes
5°C (41°F)
8 hours
Load the sealer gun with the cartridge.
Apply adhesive to the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
Specification
Area
Measurement
a
12.0 mm (0.472 in.)
b
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
c
25.1 mm (0.988 in.)
d
14.1 mm (0.555 in.)
e
11.5 mm (0.452 in.)
Install the back door glass to the vehicle body.
Lightly press the front surface of the back door glass to ensure that the back door glass is securely fit to the vehicle body.
NOTICE:
Check that the spacers are attached to the vehicle body correctly.
Check that the vehicle body and back door glass have a small gap between them.
Hold the back door glass in place securely with protective tape or equivalent until the adhesive hardens.
NOTICE:
Do not drive the vehicle for the amount of time written in the table below.
Minimum time
Temperature
Minimum time prior to driving vehicle
35°C (95°F)
1 hour 30 minutes
20°C (68°F)
5 hours
5°C (41°F)
24 hours
5. CHECK FOR LEAKS AND REPAIR
Conduct a leak test after the adhesive has completely hardened.
6. INSTALL REAR DOOR TRIM BOARD SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Engage the 19 clips and claw, and install the rear door trim board sub-assembly.
Using a "torx" socket wrench, install the 2 "torx" screws.
7. INSTALL SLIDE DOOR WINDOW GARNISH LH
Engage the clips and install the slide door window garnish.

---

### FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS > REMOVAL

FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE SLIDE DOOR WINDOW GARNISH LH
Disengage the 4 clips and remove the slide door window garnish.
2. REMOVE REAR DOOR TRIM BOARD SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Using a "torx" socket wrench (T25), remove the 2 "torx" screws.
Disengage the 19 clips and claw and remove the rear door trim board sub-assembly.
3. REMOVE REAR DOOR TRIM MOULDING NO.2 LH
Remove the rear door trim moulding No.2 LH as shown in the illustration.
4. REMOVE FRONT SIDE WINDOW GLASS
Apply protective tape to the outer surface of the vehicle body to prevent scratches.
From the interior, insert piano wires between the vehicle body and back door glass as shown in the illustration.
Tie objects that can serve as handles (for example, wooden blocks) to all wire ends.
NOTICE:
When separating the back door glass from the vehicle, be careful not to damage the vehicles paint or interior/exterior ornaments.
Cut through the adhesive by pulling the piano wire around the back door glass.
Using a suction cup, remove the back door glass.
NOTICE:
Leave as much adhesive on the vehicle body as possible when removing the back door glass.

### BACK DOOR GLASS > INSTALLATION

BACK DOOR GLASS > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. CLEAN BACK DOOR GLASS
Using a scraper, remove the spacers, moulding and adhesive from the back door glass.
Clean the outer edge of the back door glass with non-residue solvent.
NOTICE:
Do not touch the back door glass surface after cleaning it.
Even if using new back door glass, clean the back door glass with non-residue solvent.
2. CLEAN VEHICLE BODY
Clean and shape the contact surface of the vehicle body.
On the contact surface of the vehicle body, use a knife to cut away excess adhesive as shown in the illustration.
HINT:
Leave as much adhesive on the vehicle body as possible.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the vehicle body.
Clean the contact surface of the vehicle body with cleaner.
HINT:
Even if all the adhesive has been removed, clean the vehicle body.
3. INSTALL BACK WINDOW GLASS CLIP LH
Apply Primer G to the glass where the clip will be installed.
HINT:
If primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Install a new clip onto the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
4. INSTALL BACK WINDOW GLASS CLIP RH
Apply Primer G to the glass where the clip will be installed.
HINT:
If primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Install a new clip onto the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
5. INSTALL BACK DOOR GLASS SPACER NO.1
Apply Primer G to the glass where the spacers will be installed.
HINT:
If primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Install 2 new spacers onto the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
6. INSTALL BACK WINDOW GLASS SPACER NO.2
Apply Primer G to the glass where the spacers will be installed.
HINT:
If primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Install 2 new spacers onto the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
7. INSTALL BACK DOOR GLASS
Using a brush or sponge, apply Primer M to the exposed part of the vehicle body.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer M to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Do not apply Primer M to the adhesive.
Throw away any leftover Primer M.
Do not apply too much Primer M.
Using a brush or sponge, apply Primer G to the contact surface of the back door glass.
HINT:
If Primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Apply adhesive to the back door glass.
Adhesive:
Toyota Genuine Windshield Glass Adhesive or equivalent
Cut off the tip of cartridge nozzle as shown in the illustration.
Specification:
Area
Measurement
a
12.0 mm (0.472 in.)
b
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
HINT:
After cutting off the tip, use all adhesive within the time written in the table below.
Usage timeframe
Temperature
Usage Timeframe
35°C (95°F)
15 minutes
20°C (68°F)
1 hour 40 minutes
5°C (41°F)
8 hours
Load the sealer gun with the cartridge.
Apply adhesive to the back door glass as shown in the illustration.
Specification
Area
Measurement
a
12.0 mm (0.472 in.)
b
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
c
13.0 mm (0.511 in.)
Install the back door glass to the vehicle body.
Lightly press the front surface of the back door glass to ensure that the back door glass is securely fit to the vehicle body.
NOTICE:
Check that the spacers are attached to the vehicle body correctly.
Check that the vehicle body and back door glass have a small gap between them.
Hold the back door glass in place securely with protective tape or equivalent until the adhesive hardens.
NOTICE:
Do not drive the vehicle for the amount of time written in the table below.
Minimum time
Temperature
Minimum time prior to driving vehicle
35°C (95°F)
1 hour 30 minutes
20°C (68°F)
5 hours
5°C (41°F)
24 hours
8. CHECK FOR LEAKS AND REPAIR
Conduct a leak test after the adhesive has completely hardened.
Seal any leaks with auto glass sealer.
9. INSTALL REAR WIPER MOTOR ASSEMBLY
Install the grommet as shown in the illustration.
Install the rear wiper motor with the 3 bolts.
Torque:
5.4 N*m{
55 kgf*cm
, 48 in.*lbf
}
Install the clamp and connect the connector.
10. INSTALL BACK DOOR TRIM BOARD ASSEMBLY UPPER
Engage the clips and install the back door trim board assembly upper.
11. INSTALL REAR WIPER LINK PIVOT WASHER NO.1
Install the rear No.1 wiper link pivot washer from the outside of the vehicle with the installation mark at the top.
Using the socket wrench (27 mm), install the lock nut.
Torque:
12 N*m{
120 kgf*cm
, 9 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Do not damage the glass.
12. INSTALL REAR WIPER SHAFT COVER
13. INSTALL REAR WIPER ARM
Operate the wiper, and stop the rear wiper motor at the automatic stop position.
Clean the wiper arm serrations.
Clean the wiper pivot serrations with a wire brush (when reinstalling).
Install the rear wiper arm and blade assembly with the nut at the position as shown in the illustration.
Torque:
9.8 N*m{
100 kgf*cm
, 87 in.*lbf
}
HINT:
Hold the arm hinge by hand to fasten the nut.
Operate the rear wiper while spraying water or washer fluid on the windshield. Make sure that the wipers function properly and there is no interference with the vehicle body.

### BACK DOOR GLASS > REMOVAL

BACK DOOR GLASS > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE REAR WIPER ARM
Disengage the 3 claws and separate the head cap as shown in the illustration.
Remove the rear wiper arm nut and rear wiper arm and blade assembly.
2. REMOVE REAR WIPER SHAFT COVER
Remove the rear wiper shaft cover.
3. REMOVE REAR WIPER LINK PIVOT WASHER NO.1
Using the socket wrench (27 mm), remove the lock nut and rear No.1 wiper link pivot washer.
NOTICE:
Do not damage the glass.
4. REMOVE BACK DOOR TRIM BOARD ASSEMBLY UPPER
Disengage the 6 clips and remove the back door trim board assembly upper.
Remove the remaining clips on the body, and reinstall them to the back door trim board assembly upper.
5. REMOVE REAR WIPER MOTOR ASSEMBLY
Disconnect the connector and remove the clamp.
Remove the 3 bolts and rear wiper motor and grommet as a unit.
Remove the grommet from the rear wiper motor.
6. REMOVE BACK DOOR GLASS
Apply protective tape to the outer surface of the vehicle body to prevent scratches.
From the interior, insert piano wires between the vehicle body and back door glass as shown in the illustration.
Tie objects that can serve as handles (for example, wooden blocks) to all wire ends.
NOTICE:
When separating the back door glass from the vehicle, be careful not to damage the vehicles paint or interior/exterior ornaments.
Cut through the adhesive by pulling the piano wire around the back door glass.
Using a suction cup, remove the back door glass.
NOTICE:
Leave as much adhesive on the vehicle body as possible when removing the back door glass.

---

### WIPER SWITCH > INSTALLATION

WIPER SWITCH > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL WIPER SWITCH
Install the wiper switch with the claw.
HINT:
Slide the steering column cover upper upward to install the wiper switch.
Connect the connector.
2. INSTALL STEERING COLUMN COVER LOWER
Engage the 3 claws to install the steering column cover upper and lower with the 2 "torx" screws.
Torque:
1.5 N*m{
15 kgf*cm
, 13 in.*lbf
}
3. PLACE FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
4. CENTER SPIRAL CABLE
Check that the ignition switch is off.
Check that the battery negative (-) terminal is disconnected.
CAUTION:
After removing the terminal, wait for at least 90 seconds before starting the operation.
Rotate the spiral cable with steering sensor counterclockwise slowly by hand until it feels firm.
NOTICE:
Do not turn the spiral cable with steering sensor by the airbag wire harness.
Rotate the spiral cable with steering sensor clockwise approximately 2.5 turns to align the marks.
NOTICE:
Do not turn the spiral cable with steering sensor by the airbag wire harness.
HINT:
The spiral cable with steering sensor will rotate approximately 2.5 turns to both the left and right from the center.
5. INSTALL STEERING WHEEL ASSEMBLY
w/o AIRBAG:
Position the horn contact plate so that the projecting parts are as shown the illustration.
Align the matchmarks on the steering wheel assembly and the steering column assembly.
Install the nut.
Torque:
50 N*m{
510 kgf*cm
, 37 ft.*lbf
}
Connect the connectors.
6. INSPECT STEERING WHEEL CENTER POINT
7. INSTALL STEERING PAD (w/ AIRBAG)
Support the steering pad with one hand.
Connect the connector to the steering pad.
NOTICE:
When handling the airbag connector, take care not to damage the airbag wire harness.
Connect the horn connector.
Confirm that the circumference groove of the "torx" screw fits in the screw case, and place the steering pad onto the steering wheel assembly.
Using a "torx" socket wrench (T30), tighten the 2 "torx" screws.
Torque:
8.8 N*m{
90 kgf*cm
, 78 in.*lbf
}
8. INSTALL NO.3 STEERING WHEEL COVER LOWER (w/ AIRBAG)
Install the steering wheel cover lower No.3.
9. INSTALL NO.2 STEERING WHEEL COVER LOWER (w/ AIRBAG)
Install the steering wheel cover lower No.2.
10. INSPECT STEERING PAD (w/ AIRBAG)
With the steering pad installed on the vehicle, perform a visual check. If there are any defects as mentioned below, replace the steering pad with a new one:
Cuts, minute cracks or marked discoloration on the steering pad top surface or in the grooved portion.
Make sure that the horn sounds.
HINT:
If the horn does not sound, inspect the horn system.
11. INSTALL STEERING PAD (w/o AIRBAG)
Connect the horn connector.
Install the steering pad with the screw.
Torque:
1.5 N*m{
15 kgf*cm
, 13 in.*lbf
}
12. CONNECT CABLE TO BATTERY NEGATIVE TERMINAL
13. PERFORM INITIALIZATION
(
Click here
)
14. INSPECT SUPPLEMENTAL RESTRAINT SYSTEM WARNING LIGHT
(
Click here
)

If the result is not as specified, replace the wiper switch.
Intermittent Operation Check
Connect the voltmeter (+) terminal to terminal 7 (+1) and the voltmeter (-) terminal to terminal 2 (E).
Connect the battery's positive (+) lead to terminal 17 (+B), and connect the battery's negative (-) lead to terminals 2 (E) and 16 (+S).
Turn the wiper switch to the INT position.
Connect the battery's positive (+) lead to terminal 16 (+S) for 5 seconds.
Connect the battery's negative (-) lead to terminal 16 (+S) to operate the intermittent wiper relay. Measure the voltage between terminals 7 (+1) and 2 (E).
OK:
Refer to the illustration below.
If the result is not as specified, replace the wiper switch.
Front Washer Operation Check
Turn the wiper switch to the OFF position.
Connect the battery's positive (+) lead to terminal 17 (+B), connect the battery's negative (-) to terminals 16 (+S) and 2 (E).
Connect the voltmeter (+) terminal to terminal 7 (+1) and the voltmeter (-) terminal to terminal 2 (E). Measure the voltage between terminals 7 (+1) and 2 (E) when turning the washer switch ON and OFF respectively.
OK:
Refer to the illustration.
If the result is not as specified, replace the wiper switch.
2. INSPECT WIPER SWITCH (RHD)

If result is not as specified, replace the wiper switch.
Intermittent Operation Check
Connect the voltmeter (+) terminal to terminal 3 (+1) and the voltmeter (-) terminal to terminal 8 (E).
Connect the battery's positive (+) lead to terminal 11 (+B), and connect the battery's negative (-) lead to terminals 8 (E) and 12 (+S).
Turn the wiper switch to the INT position.
Connect the battery's positive (+) lead to terminal 12 (+S) for 5 seconds.
Connect the battery's negative (-) lead to terminal 12 (+S) to operate the intermittent wiper relay. Measure the voltage between terminals 3 (+1) and 8 (E).
OK:
Refer to the illustration below.
If the result is not as specified, replace the wiper switch.
Front Washer Operation Check
Turn the wiper switch to the OFF position.
Connect the battery's positive (+) lead to terminal 11 (+B), and connect the battery's negative (-) to terminals 12 (+S) and 8 (E).
Connect the voltmeter (+) terminal to terminal 3 (+) and the voltmeter (-) terminal to terminal 8 (E). Measure the voltage between terminals 3 (+1) and 8 (E) when turning the washer switch ON and OFF respectively.
OK:
Refer to the illustration.
If the result is not as specified, replace the wiper switch.

### WIPER SWITCH > REMOVAL

WIPER SWITCH > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. DISCONNECT CABLE FROM NEGATIVE BATTERY TERMINAL
(
Click here
)
2. INSPECT FRONT WHEELS FACING STRAIGHT AHEAD
3. REMOVE STEERING PAD (w/o AIRBAG)
Remove the screw.
Remove the steering pad.
Disconnect the horn connector.
4. REMOVE NO.2 STEERING WHEEL COVER LOWER (w/ AIRBAG)
Using a screwdriver, remove the steering wheel No.2 cover lower.
HINT:
Tape up the screwdriver tip before use.
5. REMOVE NO.3 STEERING WHEEL COVER LOWER (w/ AIRBAG)
Using a screwdriver, remove the steering wheel No.3 cover lower.
HINT:
Tape up the screwdriver tip before use.
6. REMOVE STEERING PAD (w/ AIRBAG)
Using a "torx" socket wrench (T30), loosen the 2 "torx" screws until the groove along the screw circumference catches on the screw case.
Pull out the steering pad from the steering wheel assembly and support the steering pad with one hand.
Disconnect the horn connector.
Disconnect the connector and remove the steering pad.
NOTICE:
When handling the airbag connector, take care not to damage the airbag wire harness.
7. REMOVE STEERING WHEEL ASSEMBLY
Remove the steering wheel set nut.
Put matchmarks on the steering wheel assembly and steering main shaft assembly.
Using SST, remove the steering wheel assembly.
SST
09950-50013
(09951-05010, 09952-05010, 09953-05020, 09954-05021)
8. REMOVE STEERING COLUMN COVER LOWER
Set the steering tilt mechanism to the lowermost position.
Remove the 2 "torx" screws from the steering column cover lower.
Press on the left side of the steering column cover lower to disengage claw A.
Press on the right side of the steering column cover lower to disengage claw B.
HINT:
If it is difficult to disengage claw B, enlarge the clearance downward on the left side of the steering column cover lower to disengage the claw.
Twist the steering column cover lower by pulling it downward.
NOTICE:
Be sure to pull the steering column cover lower downward to prevent damage to the claw.
9. REMOVE WIPER SWITCH
Disconnect the connector.
Disengage the claw and remove the wiper switch as shown in the illustration.
NOTICE:
If the claw is pushed with excessive force, it may be broken.
HINT:
Slide the steering column cover upper upward to remove the wiper switch.

### FRONT WIPER MOTOR > INSPECTION

FRONT WIPER MOTOR > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT FRONT WIPER MOTOR (LHD)
Apply battery voltage to the front wiper motor connector.
Check operation of the front wiper motor.
OK:
Measurement condition
Specified condition
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 1 (+1)
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 5 (E)
Motor operates at low speed
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 4 (+2)
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 5 (E)
Motor operates at high speed
If the result is not as specified, replace the front wiper motor.
2. INSPECT FRONT WIPER MOTOR (RHD)
Apply battery voltage to the wiper motor connector.
Check operation of the front wiper motor.
OK:
Measurement condition
Specified condition
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 5 (+1)
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 4 (E)
Motor operates at low speed
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 3 (+2)
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 4 (E)
Motor operates at high speed
If the result is not as specified, replace the front wiper motor.

for Preparation
Click here

Remove the rear wiper shaft cover.
3. REMOVE REAR NO.1 WIPER LINK PIVOT WASHER
Using the socket wrench (27 mm), remove the lock nut and rear No.1 wiper link pivot washer.
NOTICE:
Do not damage the glass.
4. REMOVE BACK DOOR TRIM BOARD ASSEMBLY UPPER
Disengage the 6 clips and remove the back door trim board assembly upper.
Remove the remaining clips on the body, and reinstall them to the back door trim board assembly upper.
5. REMOVE REAR WIPER MOTOR
Disconnect the connector and remove the clamp.
Remove the 3 bolts and rear wiper motor and grommet as a unit.
Remove the grommet from the rear wiper motor.

---

### REAR WIPER MOTOR > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

REAR WIPER MOTOR > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT REAR WIPER MOTOR
Operate the rear wiper motor.
Stop the rear wiper motor operation.
Check the automatic stop position.
OK:
The rear wiper stops at the position shown in the illustration.
If the result is not as specified, the rear wiper motor may have a malfunction.

---

### REAR WIPER RUBBER > INSTALLATION

REAR WIPER RUBBER > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL REAR WIPER RUBBER
Install the 2 rear wiper rubber backing plates to the rear wiper rubber.
NOTICE:
Be sure to install the rear wiper rubber backing plates in the correct direction.
Install the rear wiper rubber so that the head part (wide side) of the rear wiper rubber faces the wiper pivot axle side.
NOTICE:
Push the rear wiper blade into the grooves of the rear wiper rubber to engage them completely.
2. INSTALL REAR WIPER BLADE

---

### REAR WIPER RUBBER > REMOVAL

REAR WIPER RUBBER > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE REAR WIPER BLADE
Remove the rear wiper blade from the rear wiper arm.
NOTICE:
Do not bend the rear wiper arm with the rear wiper blade removed, as the arm tip could damage the windshield surface.
2. REMOVE REAR WIPER RUBBER
Remove the rear wiper rubber from the rear wiper blade.
Remove the 2 rear wiper rubber backing plates from the rear wiper rubber.

---

### FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Hi-back Seat Type) > INSTALLATION

FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Hi-back Seat Type) > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY
Connect the front seat inner belt assembly connector and install the front seat assembly.
Align the front seat assembly adjuster pin with the holes in the body.
Move the front seat assembly to the rearmost position.
NOTICE:
Make sure that the front seat assembly is securely locked.
Temporarily tighten the 2 bolts on the front side of the front seat assembly.
Move the front seat assembly fully forward.
NOTICE:
Make sure that the front seat assembly is securely locked.
Temporarily tighten the 2 bolts on the rear side of the front seat assembly.
Move the front seat assembly to the rearmost position.
NOTICE:
Make sure that the front seat assembly is securely locked.
Fully tighten the 2 bolts on the front side of the front seat assembly in the order of outer and inner side.
Torque:
39 N*m{
398 kgf*cm
, 29 ft.*lbf
}
Move the front seat assembly fully forward.
NOTICE:
Make sure that the front seat assembly is securely locked.
Fully tighten the 2 bolts on the rear side of the front seat assembly in the order of outer and inner side.
Torque:
39 N*m{
398 kgf*cm
, 29 ft.*lbf
}

---

### FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Hi-back Seat Type) > REMOVAL

FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Hi-back Seat Type) > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY
Move the front seat assembly fully forward.
Remove the 2 bolts on the rear side of the seat.
Move the front seat assembly to the rearmost position.
Remove the 2 bolts on the front side of the seat.
Move the front seat assembly to the center of the seat slide rail. Set the seatback in the upright position.
Disconnect the front seat inner belt assembly connector.
Remove the front seat assembly.

---

### FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Hi-back Seat Type) > DISASSEMBLY

FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Hi-back Seat Type) > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER RELEASE HANDLE
Using a screwdriver, disengage the claw and remove the reclining adjuster release handle.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
2. REMOVE FRONT SEAT CUSHION SHIELD INNER NO.1 LH
Disengage the 2 claws and remove the 2 screws and front seat cushion shield inner No.1 LH.
3. REMOVE FRONT SEAT CUSHION SHIELD INNER LH
Disengage the front seat inner belt assembly wire.
Disengage the 2 claws and remove the 2 screws and front seat cushion shield inner LH.
4. REMOVE FRONT SEAT INNER BELT ASSEMBLY
for Driver side:
Disengage the 3 clamps.
for Passenger side:
Disconnect the connector.
Disengage the 5 clamps.
Remove the bolt and front seat inner belt assembly.
5. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEAT CUSHION COVER WITH PAD
Remove the 2 clips and 2 hog rings.
Disengage the hook and remove the separate type front seat cushion cover with pad.
6. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEAT CUSHION COVER
Remove the 7 hog rings and separate type front seat cushion cover.
7. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEAT CUSHION PAD
8. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEATBACK COVER WITH PAD
Disengage the hooks and remove the separate type front seatback cover with pad.
9. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEATBACK COVER
Remove the 5 hog rings and separate type front seatback cover.
10. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEATBACK PAD
11. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER INSIDE COVER LH
Disengage the 2 claws and remove the screw and reclining adjuster inside cover LH.
12. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER INSIDE COVER RH
HINT:
Use the same procedures for the RH side and LH side.
13. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER INSIDE COVER LH
Disengage the claw and remove the screw and reclining adjuster inside cover LH.
14. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER INSIDE COVER RH
HINT:
Use the same procedures for the RH side and LH side.
15. REMOVE FRONT SEAT WITH ADJUSTER FRAME ASSEMBLY

### FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Split Bench Seat Type) > INSTALLATION

FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Split Bench Seat Type) > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL FRONT SEATBACK CENTER ASSEMBLY
Install the front seatback center assembly with the 4 bolts.
Torque:
39 N*m{
398 kgf*cm
, 29 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Tighten the bolts in the following order:
Temporarily tighten bolt (1).
Fully tighten bolt (2).
Fully tighten bolt (1).
Fully tighten bolt (3).
Fully tighten bolt (4).
2. INSTALL FRONT SEATBACK ASSEMBLY
Install the front seatback assembly with the 4 bolts.
Torque:
39 N*m{
398 kgf*cm
, 29 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Tighten the bolts in the order indicated in the illustration.
3. INSTALL SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEAT CUSHION COVER WITH PAD
w/o Seat belt warning occupant detection sensor:
Install the separate type front seat cushion cover with pad with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
39 N*m{
398 kgf*cm
, 29 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Make sure that the wire frame of the separate type front seat cushion cover is completely engaged.
Tighten the bolts in the following order.
Temporarily tighten bolt (2).
Fully tighten bolt (1).
Fully tighten bolt (2).
w/ Seat belt warning occupant detection sensor:
Connect the connector.
Install the separate type front seat cushion cover with pad with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
39 N*m{
398 kgf*cm
, 29 ft.*lbf
}
NOTICE:
Make sure that the wire frame of the separate type front seat cushion cover is completely engaged.
Tighten the bolts in the following order.
Temporarily tighten bolt (2).
Fully tighten bolt (1).
Fully tighten bolt (2).

FRONT SEAT ASSEMBLY (for Split Bench Seat Type) > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE SEPARATE TYPE FRONT SEAT CUSHION COVER
Remove the 23 hog rings.
Remove the 9 hog rings and separate type front seat cushion cover.

Remove the 5 hog rings and separate type front seatback cover.

, 30 ft.*lbf
}

16 N*m{

Operate the air conditioning system.
Disconnect the pressure sensor connector.

Check the voltage between terminals 2 and 1 of the pressure sensor.
Standard voltage:
The voltage varies with the refrigerant pressure as shown in the chart.
If the voltage value is not as specified, replace the pressure sensor.

### CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > PRECAUTION

CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > PRECAUTION
for Preparation
Click here
1.WIRE HARNESS REPAIR
After repairing the wire bus line with a solder, wrap the repaired part with vinyl tape.
NOTICE:
The CANL bus line and CANH bus line must be installed together at all times.
When installing, make sure to braid them.
The difference in length between the CANL bus line and CANH bus line should be within 100 mm (3.93 in.).
Leave approximately 80 mm (3.15 in.) loose in the braided wires around the connector.
Do not use a bypass wire between the connectors.
NOTICE:
If you use a bypass wire, the characteristic of the braided wire harness will be lost.
2.CONNECTOR HANDLING
When checking resistance with a tester, insert the tester probes from the backside (harness side) of the connector.
If it is impossible to check resistance from the back of the connector, use a repair wire to check it.

### CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
BRIEF DESCRIPTION
CAN (Controller Area Network) is a serial data communication system for real time application. It is an in-vehicle multiplex communication system which has a high communication speed (500 kbps) and a function to detect malfunctions.
The CAN performs communication based on differential voltage by pairing the CANH (CAN+) bus line and CANL (CAN-) bus line.
The CAN has two resistors of 120 Ω that are necessary for communication with the main bus line.
DEFINITION OF TERMS
Main bus line
The main bus line is a wire harness between the two terminus circuits on the bus (communication line). This is the main bus in the CAN communication system.
Sub bus line
The sub bus line is a wire harness which diverges from the main bus line to an ECU or sensor.
Terminus circuit
The terminus circuit is a circuit which is placed to convert communication current of the CAN communication into bus voltage. It consists of a resistor and condenser. Two terminus circuits are necessary for a bus.
CAN J/C
The CAN J/C is a junction designed for CAN communication, which stores a terminus circuit.
COMPONENTS WHICH COMMUNICATE THROUGH THE COMMUNICATION SYSTEM
ECM (2TR-FE, 1KD-FTV, 2KD-FTV)
Air Conditioning Amplifier
Transmission Control ECU (*1)
HINT:
(*1): Applicable only to automatic transmission vehicles with 1KD-FTV or 2KD-FTVengine.
The CAN bus line between the ECM and the air conditioning amplifier is different from the CAN bus line between the transmission control ECU and CAN bus (
Click here
).
DIAGNOSTIC CODES FOR THE CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM
The DTC for the CAN communication system is as follows:
U0001/A2
HINT:
Applicable only to automatic transmission vehicles with 2KD-FTV engine.
REMARKS FOR TROUBLESHOOTING
Trouble in the CAN bus (communication line) can be checked from the DLC3 (except a wire break in lines other than the DLC3 sub bus line).
The CAN communication system cannot detect trouble in the DLC3 sub bus line even though the DLC3 is also connected to the CAN communication system.
HOW TO DISTINGUISH THE J/C CONNECTOR
In the CAN communication system, all connectors connected to the CAN J/C are the same shape. The connectors connected to the CAN J/C can be distinguished by the colors of the bus lines and the connecting side of the connector.
HINT:
Refer to "TERMINALS OF ECU" (
Click here
) for bus line colors or the type of connecting surface.

---

Symptom
Trouble Area
Resistance between terminals 6 (CANH) and 14 (CANL) of the DLC3 is more than 69 Ω.
CAN main bus line
ECM
CAN J/C
WIRING DIAGRAM
INSPECTION PROCEDURE

Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.

Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Value
Result
H23-6 (CANH) - H23-14 (CANL)
IG switch OFF
108 to 132 Ω
A
H23-6 (CANH) - H23-14 (CANL)
IG switch OFF
132 Ω or higher
B
NOTICE:
When the measured value is 132 Ω or higher and air conditioning control functions (cooling, heating, and dehumidification) do not operate normally, there may be a fault other than a disconnection in the DLC3 sub bus line. For that reason, troubleshooting should be performed again from "HOW TO PROCEED WITH TROUBLESHOOTING (
Click here
)" after repairing the trouble area.
B
REPAIR OR REPLACE DLC3 SUB BUS LINE OR CONNECTOR (CAN-H, CAN-L)
A

Disconnect the CAN main bus line connector (H4) from the CAN J/C.

Standard resistance:
Between the terminals where the connector (H4) was connected
Condition
Specified value
CANH - CANL
IG switch OFF
108 to 132 Ω
NG
REPLACE CAN J/C
OK
3.INSPECT ECM

Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
2TR-FE:
Tester connection

B7-34 (CANL)
IG switch OFF
108 to 132 Ω
1KD-FTV, 2KD-FTV:
Tester connection
Condition

B7-23 (CANL)
IG switch OFF
108 to 132 Ω
NG
REPLACE ECM
OK

### CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > DIAGNOSIS SYSTEM

CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > DIAGNOSIS SYSTEM
for Preparation
Click here
BUS CHECK (COMMUNICATION MALFUNCTION DTC)
HINT:
Only CAN communication system DTCs for each ECU can be displayed on the intelligent tester.
Select "Bus Check" from the "System Select" screen on the intelligent tester.
Select "Communication Malfunction DTC" from the "Bus Check" screen, and then select "Enter".
Select a system to be checked and select "Details".
CAN communication system DTC(s) is/are displayed.
BUS CHECK (COMMUNICATION BUS CHECK)
HINT:
The ECUs and sensors that are properly connected to the CAN communication system can be displayed using the intelligent tester.
Select "Communication Bus Check" from the "Bus Check" screen.
The screen displays the ECUs and sensors that are properly connected to the CAN communication system.
HINT:
There is a communication stop in the system of any properly connected ECUs or sensors that are not displayed.
"There is no system connected to the communication bus" is displayed if there are no ECUs or sensors connected to the CAN bus.
CHECK FOR INSTALLED SYSTEMS (ECUS) THAT ADOPT CAN COMMUNICATION
Systems (ECUs) that adopt CAN communication vary depending on the vehicle's optional settings. Check which systems (ECUs) are installed on the vehicle.
ECU name
Check method
ECM
Installed on all vehicles. (CAN communication is adopted only for vehicles with the 2TR-FE, 1KD-FTV or 2KD-FTV engine.)
Air conditioning amplifier
Installed on vehicles with air conditioners. (CAN communication is adopted only for vehicles with 2TR-FE, 1KD-FTV or 2KD-FTV engine.)
Transmission control ECU
Installed on only automatic transmission vehicles with 1KD-FTV or 2KD-FTV engine.

### CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > FAIL-SAFE CHART

CAN COMMUNICATION SYSTEM > FAIL-SAFE CHART
for Preparation
Click here
FAIL-SAFE FUNCTION
The fail-safe function is designed to prevent malfunctions in various systems by switching the systems to fail-safe modes specified for each system, if communication fails in any of the CAN bus lines (communication lines) due to a short circuit or other causes.
Effects on each system when communication is impossible (for further details, see the pages for each system):
Function
ECM
Air Conditioning Amplifier
Action when communication is impossible
DTC detection (Driver detectable)
Compressor control (Air conditioning control = cooling, heating and dehumidification)
Tx
Rx
Air conditioning function (cooling and dehumidification) stops. (Compressor stops.)
None (Light does not come on.)
PTC heater control (Heating control)
Tx
Rx
Heating function stops. (PTC heater drive stops.)
None (Light does not come on.)
HINT:
Tx: Transmission to each ECU
Rx: Reception from each ECU

### SLIDE DOOR CONTROL RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > INSTALLATION

SLIDE DOOR CONTROL RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here

### SLIDE DOOR CONTROL RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > REMOVAL

SLIDE DOOR CONTROL RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE REAR DOOR SCUFF PLATE
Disengage the 11 clips and remove the rear door scuff plate.
2. REMOVE DOOR CONTROL RELAY
Remove the bolt and the door control relay.

---

### SLIDE DOOR CLOSER RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > INSTALLATION

SLIDE DOOR CLOSER RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL SLIDE DOOR CLOSER RELAY
Install the slide door closer relay with the bolt.
2. INSTALL SLIDE DOOR SERVICE HOLE NO.1 COVER
Install the slide door service hole No.1 cover.
Connect the cable.
Engage the clamp.
3. INSTALL REAR DOOR TRIM BOARD SUB-ASSEMBLY
Engage the 19 clips and claw and install the rear door trim board sub-assembly.
Using a "torx" socket wrench, install the 2 "torx" screws.
4. INSTALL SLIDE DOOR WINDOW GARNISH
Engage the 4 clips and install the slide door window garnish.

---

### SLIDE DOOR CLOSER RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > REMOVAL

SLIDE DOOR CLOSER RELAY (w/o Power Slide Door) > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE SLIDE DOOR WINDOW GARNISH
Disengage the 4 clips and remove the slide door window garnish.
2. REMOVE REAR DOOR TRIM BOARD SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using a "torx" socket wrench (T25), remove the 2 "torx" screws.
Disengage the 19 clips and claw, and remove the rear door trim board sub-assembly.
3. REMOVE SLIDE DOOR SERVICE HOLE NO.1 COVER
Disengage the clamp.
Disconnect the cable from the slide door lock remote control assembly.
Remove the slide door service hole No.1 cover.
4. REMOVE SLIDE DOOR CLOSER RELAY
Disconnect the connector.
Remove the bolt and the slide door closer relay.

---

### DOOR CONTROL RECEIVER > INSTALLATION

DOOR CONTROL RECEIVER > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL DOOR CONTROL RECEIVER
Install the door control receiver with the bolt.
Connect the connector.
2. INSTALL QUARTER TRIM PANEL ASSEMBLY REAR (High Roof Type)
3. INSTALL QUARTER TRIM PANEL ASSEMBLY REAR (Middle Roof Type)
4. INSTALL BACK DOOR SCUFF PLATE

---

### DOOR CONTROL RECEIVER > REMOVAL

DOOR CONTROL RECEIVER > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE BACK DOOR SCUFF PLATE
Using a screwdriver, disengage the 7 clips and 4 claws and remove the back door scuff plate.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
2. REMOVE QUARTER TRIM PANEL ASSEMBLY REAR (Middle Roof Type)
Using a screwdriver, disengage the 21 clips and remove the quarter trim panel assembly rear LH.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
3. REMOVE QUARTER TRIM PANEL ASSEMBLY REAR (High Roof Type)
Using a screwdriver, disengage the 35 clips and remove the quarter trim panel assembly rear LH.
HINT:
Tape the screwdriver tip before use.
4. REMOVE DOOR CONTROL RECEIVER
Disconnect the connector.
Remove the bolt and the door control receiver.

, 29 ft.*lbf
}

, 29 ft.*lbf
}

Install the rear No. 1 seat leg sub-assembly with the 2 E-rings and 2 pins.
Install the 2 springs.

, 31 ft.*lbf

### REAR NO. 2 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > DISASSEMBLY

REAR NO. 2 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here

Separate the 2 hook and loop fasteners and remove the sub seat back cover sub-assembly.

Remove the screw and sub seat cushion frame cushion.
8. REMOVE NO. 1 SUB SEAT CUSHION BOARD PLATE (w/ Sub Seat)
Remove the rear seat cover cap and disengage the hook.
Remove the 4 bolts and No. 1 sub seat cushion board plate.
9. REMOVE SUB SEAT LINK SPRING (w/ Sub Seat)
Remove the 4 E rings, the 4 wave washers, the 4 plate washers, and the 2 sub seat link springs.
10. REMOVE SUB SEAT CUSHION BRACKET SUB-ASSEMBLY (w/ Sub Seat)

11. REMOVE SUB SEAT CUSHION FRAME CUSHION (w/ Sub Seat)
Remove the screw and sub seat cushion frame cushion.
12. REMOVE SUB SEAT CUSHION FRAME SUB-ASSEMBLY (w/ Sub Seat)
Separate the hook and loop fastener.
Open the fastener of the sub seat cushion cover sub-assembly and remove the sub seat cushion frame sub-assembly.
13. REMOVE SUB SEAT CUSHION BOARD (w/ Sub Seat)
Remove the 3 screws and sub seat cushion board.
14. REMOVE SUB SEAT CUSHION PAD (w/ Sub Seat)
Remove the sub seat cushion pad from the sub seat cushion cover sub-assembly.
15. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER COVER RH
Remove the 2 screws and reclining adjuster cover RH.
16. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER COVER LH
Remove the 2 screws and reclining adjuster cover LH.
17. REMOVE REAR SEAT FRONT BRACKET COVER
Remove the rear seat front bracket cover.
18. REMOVE REAR SEAT LEG SIDE COVER
Remove the rear seat leg side cover.
19. REMOVE REAR SEAT BELT ASSEMBLY INNER (w/ Seat Belt)
Remove the bolt and rear seat belt assembly inner.
20. REMOVE SEAT LAP TYPE BELT ASSEMBLY NO. 1 (w/ Seat Belt)
Remove the bolt and seat lap type belt assembly No.1.
21. REMOVE NO. 2 SEAT CUSHION COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY WITH PAD
Disengage the hooks and remove the No. 2 seat cushion cover sub-assembly with pad.
22. REMOVE NO. 2 SEAT CUSHION COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY
w/o Seat belt:
Remove the 2 hog rings.
w/ Seat belt:
Remove the 4 hog rings.
Remove the 12 hog rings and No. 2 seat cushion cover sub-assembly.
23. REMOVE REAR SEAT CENTER BELT ASSEMBLY INNER (w/ Seat Belt)
Remove the bolt and rear seat center belt assembly inner.
24. REMOVE NO. 2 SEAT LEG SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 4 bolts and No. 2 seat leg sub-assembly.
25. REMOVE ASSIST GRIP PLUG (w/ Assist Grip)
Disengage the 8 claws and remove the 4 assist grip plugs.
26. REMOVE ASSIST GRIP ASSEMBLY (w/ Assist Grip)
Remove the 4 screws and 2 assist grip assemblies.
27. REMOVE NO. 2 SEAT BACK COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY
Open the fastener of the No. 2 seat back cover sub-assembly and turn back the cover.
Remove the 4 hog rings and the No. 2 seatback cover sub-assembly.

w/ Seat belt:

### REAR NO. 3 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > REASSEMBLY

REAR NO. 3 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL NO.3 SEATBACK SPRING ASSEMBLY (for Fold Down Type Seat)
2. INSTALL REAR SEATBACK LOCK ASSEMBLY (for Fold Down Type Seat)
Install the rear seatback lock assembly with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
33 N*m{
337 kgf*cm
, 24 ft.*lbf
}
Install a new clamp.
Install the rear seatback lock assembly wire.
3. INSTALL NO.3 SEATBACK PAD RH (for Fold Down Type Seat)
4. INSTALL NO.3 SEATBACK COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY RH (for Fold Down Type Seat)
Using hog ring pliers, install the No.3 seatback cover sub-assembly RH to the No.3 seatback pad with 2 new hog rings.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cover.
When installing the hog rings, take care to minimize wrinkles as much as possible.
Close the fastener of the No.3 seatback cover sub-assembly RH.
5. INSTALL REAR SEATBACK LOCK COVER RH (for Fold Down Type Seat)
6. INSTALL REAR SEATBACK LOCK CONTROL BEZEL (for Fold Down Type Seat)

NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cover.
When installing the hog rings, take care to minimize wrinkles as much as possible.
Close the fastener of the No.3 seatback cover sub-assembly LH.

Using hog ring pliers, install the No.3 seatback cover sub-assembly to the No.3 seatback pad with 6 new hog rings.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cover.
When installing the hog rings, take care to minimize wrinkles as much as possible.

14. INSTALL NO.3 SEAT LEG SUB-ASSEMBLY

41 N*m{
418 kgf*cm
, 30 ft.*lbf
}
for Fold down type seat:
Install the No.3 seat leg sub-assembly.

418 kgf*cm
, 30 ft.*lbf
}
16. INSTALL RECLINING ADJUSTER COVER (for Fold Down Type Seat)

29 N*m{
296 kgf*cm
, 21 ft.*lbf
}
18. INSTALL NO.2 SEAT LEG COVER (for Fold Down Type Seat)

, 31 ft.*lbf

### AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > INSTALLATION

AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL AIR CONDITIONER PANEL SUB-ASSEMBLY
Install the heater control base with the 2 screws.
Engage the 2 claws to install the defroster damper control cable sub-assembly.
Engage the 2 claws to install the boost ventilator control cable sub-assembly.
Install the clamp.
Install the connector.
Engage the 9 clips to install the air conditioner panel sub-assembly.
2. INSTALL INSTRUMENT PANEL FINISH PANEL LOWER CENTER
Engage the 8 claws and install the instrument panel finish panel lower center.
3. INSTALL SHIFTING HOLE COVER ASSEMBLY
Manual Transmission:
Engage the 6 claws and install the shifting hole cover assembly.
Automatic Transmission:
Engage the 8 claws and install the sifting hole cover assembly.
4. INSTALL SHIFT LEVER KNOB
5. INSTALL PARKING BRAKE HOLE COVER
Engage the 6 claws and install the parking brake hole cover.
6. INSTALL INSTRUMENT PANEL UNDER COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY NO.1
Engage the 3 claws.
Install the instrument panel No.1 under cover sub-assembly with the 2 clips.
7. INSTALL INSTRUMENT PANEL FINISH PANEL LOWER
Engage the 4 clips (B).
Install the instrument panel finish panel lower with the 2 clips (A).

---

### AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > REMOVAL

AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL FINISH PANEL LOWER
Using a clip remover, remove the 2 clips (A).
Disengage the 4 clips (B).
Disconnect the connector.
Push the positions indicated by the arrows in the illustration to separate the fuel lid lock control cable and bonnet (hood) control cable assembly from the panel.
Remove the instrument panel finish lower.
2. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL UNDER COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY NO.1
Using a clip remover, remove the 2 clips
Disengage the 3 claws and remove the instrument panel No.1 under cover sub-assembly.
3. REMOVE PARKING BRAKE HOLE COVER
Disengage the 6 claws and remove the parking brake hole cover.
4. REMOVE SHIFT LEVER KNOB
Turn the shift lever knob in the direction indicated by the arrow and remove it.
5. REMOVE SHIFTING HOLE COVER ASSEMBLY
Manual Transmission:
Disengage the 6 claws and remove the shifting hole cover assembly.
Automatic Transmission:
Disengage the 8 claws and remove the shifting hole cover assembly.
6. REMOVE INSTRUMENT PANEL FINISH PANEL LOWER CENTER
Disengage the 8 claws and remove the instrument panel finish panel lower center.
7. REMOVE AIR CONDITIONER PANEL SUB-ASSEMBLY
Release the 9 clips and the air conditioner panel sub-assembly.
Disconnect the connector and remove the air conditioner panel sub-assembly.
Release clamp.
Disengage the 2 claws and then remove the boost ventilator control cable sub-assembly.
Disengage the 2 claws and then remove the defroster damper control cable sub-assembly.
Remove the 2 screws and remove the heater control hose.

---

### AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > DISASSEMBLY

AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE HEATER CONTROL KNOB NO.2
Release the 2 claws and remove the heater control knob No.2.
2. REMOVE HEATER CONTROL OPENING COVER (w/o Heater Control Knob)
Release the 2 claws and remove the heater control opening cover.
3. REMOVE HEATER CONTROL KNOB (w/ Heater Control Knob)
Release the 2 claws and remove the heater control knob.
4. REMOVE HEATER CONTROL BASE
Release the 2 claws and remove the heater control base.
5. REMOVE HEATER CONTROL KNOB NO.3
Remove the heater control knob No.3.

AIR CONDITIONING CONTROL ASSEMBLY > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation

1. INSTALL HEATER CONTROL KNOB NO.3
Install the heater control knob No.3.
2. INSTALL HEATER CONTROL BASE
Engage the 2 claws to install the heater control base.
3. INSTALL HEATER CONTROL KNOB
Install the heater control knob.
4. INSTALL HEATER CONTROL OPENING COVER (w/o Heater Control Knob)
Engage the 2 claws to install the control opening cover.
5. INSTALL HEATER CONTROL KNOB NO.2 (w/ Heater Control Knob)
Engage the 2 claws to install the heater control knob No.2.

### REAR HEATER ASSEMBLY > INSTALLATION

REAR HEATER ASSEMBLY > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL REAR HEATER ASSEMBLY
Install the rear heater assembly with the 3 screws.
NOTICE:
Tighten the screws in the order shown in the illustration to install the rear heater assembly.
Install the 2 water hoses with the 2 clips.
2. ADD ENGINE COOLANT (2TR-FE)
Firmly tighten the drain plugs and fill the reservoir tank with coolant to the top of the inlet.
Remove the 2-way that is located near the throttle body.
When air is bled and the coolant drains out, firmly install the 2-way.
Add coolant up to the B line mark in the reservoir tank and install the radiator cap.
Coolant Capacity:
Condition
Capacity
w/ front and rear heaters
13.6 liters (14.4 US qts, 12.0 lmp. qts)
w/ front heater
11.6 liters (12.3 US qts, 10.2 lmp. qts)
w/o heater
10.6 liters (11.2 US qts, 9.3 lmp. qts)
Warm up the engine.
After the engine cools down, check that the coolant level is between the LOW and FULL level marks.
3. ADD ENGINE COOLANT (2KD-FTV)
Firmly tighten the drain plugs and fill the radiator reserve tank assembly with coolant to the top of the inlet.
Loosen the bleeder plug of outlet housing.
When air is bled and the coolant drains out, firmly install the bleeder plug.
Add coolant up to the B line mark in the radiator reserve tank assembly and install the radiator cap.
Coolant capacity:
Condition
Capacity
w/ rear heater
17.0 liters (18.0 US qts, 15.0 imp. qts)
w/o rear heater
15.0 liters (15.9 US qts, 13.2 imp. qts)
Warm up the engine.
After the engine cools down check that the coolant level is between the LOW and FULL level marks.
4. ADD ENGINE COOLANT (5L-E)
Close the engine drain plug.
Torque:
57 N*m{
581 kgf*cm
, 42 ft.*lbf
}
Close the radiator drain cock plug.
Slowly fill the cooling system with coolant.
HINT:
The coolant level inside the radiator should be abserved after pressing the radiator hose inlet and radiator hose outlet by hand..
Slowly pour coolant into the radiator reserve tank until it reaches the FULL line.
Install the radiator cap to the radiator reserve tank.
Press the inlet and outlet radiator hoses several times by hand while warming up the engine.
Stop the engine, and wait until the engine coolant cools down.
Add coolant to the FULL line of the radiator reservoir.
5. CHECK FOR ENGINE COOLANT LEAKS (2TR-FE)
CAUTION:
Do not remove the radiator cap while the engine and radiator are still hot. Pressurized, hot engine coolant and steam may be released and cause serious burns.
Fill the radiator with coolant and attach a radiator cap tester to the radiator.
Warm up the engine.
Using a radiator cap tester, increase the pressure inside the radiator to 137 kPa (1.4 kgf/cm
2
, 19.9 psi), and check that the pressure does not drop.
HINT:
If the pressure drops, check the hoses, radiator or water pump for leaks. If no external leaks are found, check the heater core, cylinder block and cylinder head.
6. CHECK FOR ENGINE COOLANT LEAKS (2KD-FTV)
CAUTION:
Do not remove the radiator cap while the engine and radiator are still hot. Pressurized, hot engine coolant and steam may be released and cause serious burns.
Fill the radiator with coolant and attach a radiator cap tester to the radiator.
Warm up the engine.
Using a radiator cap tester, increase the pressure inside the radiator to 137 kPa (1.4 kgf/cm
2
, 19.9 psi), and check that the pressure does not drop.
HINT:
If the pressure drops, check the hoses, radiator or water pump for leaks. If no external leaks are found, check the heater core, cylinder block and cylinder head.
7. CHECK FOR ENGINE COOLANT LEAKS (5L-E)
CAUTION:
Do not remove the radiator cap while the engine and radiator are still hot. Pressurized, hot engine coolant and steam may be released and cause serious burns.
Fill the radiator with coolant and attach a radiator cap tester to the radiator.
Warm up the engine.
Using a radiator cap tester, increase the pressure inside the radiator to 118 kPa (1.2 kgf/cm
2
, 17.1 psi), and check that the pressure does not drop.
HINT:
If the pressure drops, check the hoses, radiator or water pump for leaks. If no external leaks are found, check the heater core, cylinder block, and cylinder head.

### WINDOW DEFOGGER WIRE > REPAIR

WINDOW DEFOGGER WIRE > REPAIR
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT WINDOW DEFOGGER WIRE
NOTICE:
When cleaning the glass, wipe it in the direction of the wire with a soft and dry cloth. Take care not to damage the wire. Do not use detergents or glass cleaners with abrasive ingredients. When measuring voltage, wind a piece of tin foil around the tip of the tester probe and press the foil against the wire with your finger, as shown in the illustration.
Check the continuity, as the center of each defogger wire, as shown in the illustration.
2. REPAIR WINDOW DEFOGGER WIRE
Clean the broken wire tips with a grease, wax and silicone remover.
Place the masking tape along both sides of the wire to be repaired.
Thoroughly mix the repair agent (Dupont paste No.4817).
Using a fine tip brush, apply a small amount of the mixture to the wire.
After a few minutes, remove the masking the tape.
Do not repair the defogger wire for at least 24 hours afterwards.

Condition

5B-3 (L2) - Body ground

Front RH door lock key switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
5B-4 (UL2) - Body ground
Door lock key switch signal
Front RH door lock key switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*1 5B-5 (UL1) - Body ground
Door lock manual switch signal
Driver side door control switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*1 5B-6 (L1) - Body ground
Door lock manual switch signal
Driver side door control switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
5B-21 (L2) - Body ground
Door lock key switch signal
Front LH door lock key switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
5B-22 (UL2) - Body ground
Door lock key switch signal
Front LH door lock key switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*2 5B-23 (UL1) - Body ground
Door lock manual switch signal
Driver side door control switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*2 5B-24 (L1) - Body ground
Door lock manual switch signal
Driver side door control switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*3 5H-7 (UL1) - Body ground
Door lock manual switch signal
Driver side door control switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*3 5H-6 (L1) - Body ground
Door lock manual switch signal
Driver side door control switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
10 kΩ or higher → Below 1 Ω
*1: w/ Power window (RHD models), *2: w/ Power window (LHD models), *3: w/o Power window
If the result is not as specified, there may be a malfunction on the wire harness side.
Reconnect the 5A, 5B, 5E, 5H and H7 connectors.
Measure the voltage according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard voltage:
Terminal No. (Symbols)
Terminal Description
Condition
Specified Condition
5A-25 (ACT+) - Body ground
Back door lock motor drive signal
Back door control switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V → Below 1 V
5A-29 (ACT-) - Body ground
Back door lock motor drive signal
Back door control switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V → Below 1 V
5B-33 (ACT+) - Body ground
Door lock motor drive signal
Door lock control switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V → Below 1 V
5B-34 (ACT-) - Body ground
Door lock motor drive signal
Door lock control switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V → Below 1 V
5E-25 (ACT+) - Body ground
Slide door lock motor drive signal
Slide door lock control switch OFF → ON (LOCK)
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V → Below 1 V
5E-11 (ACT-) - Body ground
Slide door lock motor drive signal
Slide door lock control switch OFF → ON (UNLOCK)
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V → Below 1 V
H7-4 (ALTB) - Body ground
Power supply
Always
10 to 14 V
H7-5 (SIG) - Body ground
Power supply
Ignition switch OFF → ON
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V
H7-20 (LSWD) - Body ground
Door lock position switch signal
Driver side door lock position switch UNLOCK → LOCK
Below 1 V → 10 to 14 V
If the result is not as specified, the instrument panel J/B (body ECU) may have a malfunction.

### POWER DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

POWER DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
POWER DOOR LOCK SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
The power door lock system locks/unlocks all doors with a one-touch operation.
Check that all doors are locked when the door control switch (for manual operation) is operated to LOCK, and that all doors are unlocked when the switch is operated to UNLOCK.
Check that all doors are locked when the key in the driver's or front passenger's door key cylinder is turned to LOCK, and that all doors are unlocked when the key is turned to UNLOCK.
Functions
Outlines
Manual lock and unlock
This function can lock or unlock all doors by the door lock control switch operation.
Key-linked lock and unlock function
This function, which is linked with the key cylinder, can lock or unlock all the doors when lock or unlock operation is effected.
Door lock security function
If either of the conditions below is met, driver's door manual unlocking is prohibited.
With the ignition switch off and the driver's door closed, when the door is locked with the key-linked lock or wireless door lock.
With the ignition switch off and the driver's door locked (driver's door open and locked), when the driver's door is locked approximately 0.25 seconds after it is closed.
Key confine prevention function
When the key is inserted in the ignition key cylinder and the driver's door is open, this function does not establish manual locking or key-linked locking.
Door lock with transmitter function (w/ wireless door lock)
Pressing the door lock switch of the transmitter locks all doors.
Door unlock with transmitter function (w/ wireless door lock)
Pressing the door unlock switch of the transmitter unlocks all doors.

---

### POWER DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM > Driver Side Door UNLOCK Detection Switch Circuit

POWER DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM > Driver Side Door UNLOCK Detection Switch Circuit
for Preparation
Click here
DESCRIPTION
The door unlock detection switch is built in the door lock assembly.
This switch turns on when the door lock knob is in the unlock position and turns off when it is in the lock position. It is used as one of the operating conditions for the key confinement prevention function.
WIRING DIAGRAM
INSPECTION PROCEDURE
1.INSPECT FRONT DOOR LOCK ASSEMBLY (DOOR UNLOCK DETECTION SWITCH)
LHD Models:
Inspect the front door lock assembly.
Measure the resistance when the switch is operated according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Door Lock Condition
Specified Condition
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 1
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 4
ON (Door lock set to UNLOCK)
7 - 8 (Below 1 Ω)
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 4
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 1
OFF (Door lock set to LOCK)
7 - 8 (10 kΩ or higher)
RHD Models:
Inspect the front door lock assembly.
Measure the resistance when the switch is operated according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Door Lock Condition
Specified Condition
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 1
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 4
ON (Door lock set to UNLOCK)
7 - 8 (Below 1 Ω)
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 4
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 1
OFF (Door lock set to LOCK)
7 - 8 (10 kΩ or higher)
NG
REPLACE FRONT DOOR LOCK ASSEMBLY
OK
2.CHECK WIRE HARNESS (FRONT DOOR LOCK ASSEMBLY - INSTRUMENT PANEL J/B)
Disconnect the L1 (K1) connector.
Disconnect the instrument panel J/B connector.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
LHD Models:
Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Condition
H7-20 - L1-8
Always
Below 1 Ω
L1-7 - Body ground
Always
Below 1 Ω
RHD Models:
Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Condition
H7-20 - K1-7
Always
Below 1 Ω
K1-8 - Body ground
Always
Below 1 Ω
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK
PROCEED TO NEXT CIRCUIT INSPECTION SHOWN IN PROBLEM SYMPTOMS TABLE

### POWER DOOR LOCK CONTROL SYSTEM > Front Passenger Side Door Lock Motor Circuit

The door lock motor is built in the door lock assembly.
The body ECU receives the front passenger door lock switch signal from the master switch and operates the door lock motor.

### REAR NO. 1 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > DISASSEMBLY

REAR NO. 1 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > DISASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER COVER LH
Remove the 2 screws and reclining adjuster cover LH.
2. REMOVE RECLINING ADJUSTER COVER RH
Remove the 2 screws and reclining adjuster cover RH.
3. REMOVE REAR SEAT LEG SIDE COVER NO.3
Remove the rear seat leg side cover No.3.
HINT:
Use the same procedure for the second row.
4. REMOVE REAR SEAT LEG SIDE COVER NO.2 (for Second Row)
Remove the rear seat leg side cover No.2.
5. REMOVE REAR SEAT BELT ASSEMBLY INNER (w/ Seat Belt)
Remove the bolt and rear seat belt assembly inner.
6. REMOVE SEAT LAP TYPE BELT ASSEMBLY NO.1 (w/ Seat Belt)
Remove the bolt and seat lap type belt assembly No.1.
7. REMOVE NO.1 SEAT CUSHION COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY WITH PAD
Disengage the hooks and remove the No.1 seat cushion cover sub-assembly with pad.
8. REMOVE NO.1 SEAT CUSHION COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 hog rings.
Remove the 6 hog rings and No.1 seat cushion cover sub-assembly.
9. REMOVE NO.1 SEAT CUSHION PAD
10. REMOVE NO.1 SEAT LEG SUB-ASSEMBLY
Remove the 4 bolts and No.1 seat leg sub-assembly.
11. REMOVE ASSIST GRIP PLUG (w/ Assist Grip)
Disengage the 4 claws and remove the 2 assist grip plugs.
12. REMOVE ASSIST GRIP ASSEMBLY (w/ Assist Grip)
Remove the 2 screws and assist grip assembly.
13. REMOVE NO.1 SEATBACK COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY
Disengage the No.1 seatback cover sub-assembly hook and turn back the cover.
Remove the 2 hog rings and No.1 seatback cover sub-assembly.
14. REMOVE NO.1 SEATBACK PAD SUB-ASSEMBLY

### REAR NO. 1 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > REASSEMBLY

REAR NO. 1 SEAT ASSEMBLY (w/o Headrest) > REASSEMBLY
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL NO.1 SEATBACK PAD SUB-ASSEMBLY
2. INSTALL NO.1 SEATBACK COVER SUB-ASSEMBLY
Using hog ring pliers, install the No.1 seatback cover sub-assembly to the No.1 seatback pad sub-assembly with 2 new hog rings.

Be careful not to damage the cover.
When installing the hog rings, take care to minimize wrinkles as much as possible.

41 N*m{
418 kgf*cm
, 30 ft.*lbf
}
6. INSTALL NO.1 SEAT CUSHION PAD

NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cover.
When installing the hog rings, take care to minimize wrinkles as much as possible.
Using hog ring pliers, install the 2 new hog rings.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the cover.

Torque:

428 kgf*cm
, 31 ft.*lbf

The defogger system's thin heater wires are attached to the inside of the rear window and defog the window surface quickly.

Component

Receives rear defogger activation request signals from defogger switch and supplies power to rear window defogger
Rear Window Defogger
Receives power from DEFOG relay and heats defogger wires

### POWER WINDOW CONTROL SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

POWER WINDOW CONTROL SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
POWER WINDOW CONTROL SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
The power window control system controls the power window UP/DOWN function by using regulator motors. This system is mainly controlled by the master switch built into the driver's door and regulator switches built into passenger's door. Pressing any of these regulator switches or the driver side door window switch mounted on the master switch transmits an UP/DOWN signal to the regulator motor for the corresponding power window, which then operates the power window UP/DOWN. Pressing any switch for the power windows except the driver's power window mounted on the master switch transmits a remote UP/DOWN signal to the front passenger power window switch via the direct line.
The power window control system has the following functions:
Function
Outline
Manual up-and-down function
Function that causes window to go up while power window switch is being pulled halfway up and to go down while pushed halfway down. Window stops as soon as the switch is released.
Jam protection function
Function that automatically stops power window and moves it downward if a foreign object gets jammed in door window during auto-up operation.
Remote control function
Function that allows power window master switch to control MANUAL up-and-down operation of front passenger door window.
Key-off operation function
Function that makes it possible to operate power window for approx. 45 seconds after ignition switch is turned to ACC or LOCK position, if either front door is not opened.
Diagnosis
Function that allows the power window switch to make a diagnosis for failed section when power window switch detects a malfunction in power window system. Power window switch light comes on or blinks to inform driver.
Fail-safe
A fail-safe function to disable a part of power window functions if pulse sensor in power window motor has a malfunction:
Driver door auto up-and-down function is disabled.
Manual operation is possible by each power window switch.
CHECK WINDOW LOCK SWITCH
Check that the passenger side power window and rear power window operation is disabled when the window lock switch of the power window master switch is pressed.
Check that the passenger side power window and rear power windows can be operated when the window lock switch is pressed again.
CHECK MANUAL UP/DOWN FUNCTION
Check that the driver side power window operates as follows:
Standard:

UP (Closed)
Pushed halfway down
DOWN (Open)

HINT:
For a sign for starting operation, the wireless door lock buzzer sounds once (answer-back).
CHECK POWER WINDOW OPERATION FUNCTION AFTER IGNITION SWITCH IS TURNED OFF
When both of the following conditions are fulfilled, check that the power windows can be operated even after the ignition switch is turned off.
Within 45 seconds after the ignition switch is turned off.
The front doors are closed.
CHECK JAM PROTECTION FUNCTION
HINT:
The jam protection function prevents any part of your body from getting caught by accident between the door frame and the door glass during power window operation.
NOTICE:
If the power window motor has just been reset, raise and lower the door glass several times using MANUAL function before performing the check.
Check that the door glass goes down by approx. 50 mm (1.97 in.) right when something gets caught between the door frame and door glass during power window operation. However, when the opening between the door frame and the door glass is less than 200 mm (7.87 in.), the door glass continues to go down and does not stop until an opening of 200 mm (7.87 in.) is achieved. Operative conditions:
AUTO UP
AUTO UP function after the ignition switch is turned off
MANUAL UP function after the ignition switch is turned off
MANUAL UP function using the driver side door lock key cylinder

### POWER WINDOW CONTROL SYSTEM > Driver Side Power Window Motor Circuit

POWER WINDOW CONTROL SYSTEM > Driver Side Power Window Motor Circuit
for Preparation
Click here

HINT:
When the SRS warning light remains on and the DTC output is the normal system code, a voltage source drop is likely to occur. This malfunction is not stored in the memory by the center airbag sensor assembly. If the power source voltage returns to normal, the SRS warning light will automatically go off.
When 2 or more codes are indicated, the code with the lower number appears first.
If a code is not listed on the display chart, the center airbag sensor may have failed.
In case of any malfunction concerning an open circuit, short to ground, or short to B+ due to a squib, other trouble codes may not be detected. In this case, repair the malfunction currently indicated and then perform malfunction diagnosis again.
