### WIPER AND WASHER SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

WIPER AND WASHER SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
WASHER LINKED OPERATION
This system operates the front wipers at low speed immediately after a jet of washer fluid when the front washer switch is on for 0.3 seconds or more. The system operates the front wipers at low speed for approximately 2.2 seconds and then stops operation when the washer switch is on for 1.5 seconds or more.
INTERMITTENT OPERATION
The system operates the front wipers once in approximately 1.6 to 10.7 seconds when the front wiper switch is turned to the INT position. The intermittent time can be adjusted from 1.6 to 10.7 seconds.

### BODY > GENERAL MAINTENANCE

BODY > GENERAL MAINTENANCE
1. TIGHTEN BOLTS AND NUTS ON CHASSIS AND BODY
Where necessary, tighten all the parts of the chassis.
Front axle and suspension
Drive train
Rear axle and suspension
Brake system
Engine mounting, etc.
Where necessary, tighten all the parts of the body.
Seat belt system
Seat
Doors and hood
Body mountings
Fuel tank
Exhaust pipe system, etc.
2. REPLACE AIR CONDITIONING FILTER
Remove the glove compartment assembly.
Remove the filter case from the filter outlet.
Remove the filter from the filter case.
Replace the filter with a new one.
Installation is in the reverse order.
3. ROAD TEST
Check the engine and chassis for abnormal noises.
Check that the vehicle does not wander or pull to one side.
Check that the brakes work properly and do not drag.
Do setting of the parking brake shoes.
4. BODY INSPECTION
Check the body exterior for dents, scratches and rust.
Check the underbody for rust and damage.
If necessary, replace or repair.
5. FINAL INSPECTION
Check the operation of the body parts.
Hood
Auxiliary catch operates properly.
Hood locks securely when closed.
Front and rear doors
Door locks operate properly.
Doors close properly.
Back door
Door lock operates properly.
Seats
Seat adjusts easily and locks securely in any position.
Front seatback locks securely in any position.
Folding-down rear seatbacks lock securely.
Be sure to deliver a clean car. Especially check.
Steering wheel
Shift lever knob
All switch knobs
Door handles
Seats

### SEAT BELT WARNING SYSTEM > CUSTOMIZE PARAMETERS

SEAT BELT WARNING SYSTEM > CUSTOMIZE PARAMETERS
for Preparation
Click here
SEAT BELT BUZZER ON/OFF SETTING
The seat belt buzzer ON/OFF setting, which is a setting of the buzzer function of the combination meter, can disable the driver and front passenger side seat belt buzzers.
NOTICE:
When the buzzer OFF setting is selected, the buzzer activation while the vehicle is being driven is cancelled.
These buzzers should be on for safe driving. Perform these procedures only if it is necessary to set the buzzer off (disabled).
When either the battery cable or the combination meter connector is disconnected, these buzzers are set on (enabled).
The trip odometer returns to 0 after starting this procedure, although it is not displayed.
HINT:
"b-oFF" indicates that the buzzer is OFF. "b-on" indicates that the buzzer is ON. The seat belt buzzer ON/OFF setting will be finished (the odometer will display "ODO") if the ODO/TRIP switch is not operated for 10 seconds or more. In this case, perform step 11 to check that the buzzer ON/OFF setting is complete. If it is not complete, start from step 1 again
Driver side seat belt buzzer
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Press the ODO/TRIP switch until the odometer displays "ODO".
Turn the ignition switch off.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Press the ODO/TRIP switch immediately (within 6 seconds) and hold it down for 10 seconds or more.
Continue holding down the ODO/TRIP switch and fasten the driver side seat belt.
Check that the odometer displays either "b-on" or "b-oFF".
Press the ODO/TRIP switch to change the display to "b-oFF".
Turn the ignition switch off.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Check that no buzzer sounds when the vehicle speed is 20 km/h (12 mph) or more.
Front passenger side seat belt buzzer
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Press the ODO/TRIP switch until the odometer displays "ODO".
Turn the ignition switch off.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Sit in the front passenger seat. Press the ODO/TRIP switch immediately (within 6 seconds) and hold it down for 10 seconds or more.
Sit in the front passenger seat. Continue holding down the ODO/TRIP switch and fasten the front passenger side seat belt.
Check that the odometer displays either "b-on" or "b-oFF".
Press the ODO/TRIP switch to change the display to "b-oFF".
Turn the ignition switch off.
Turn the ignition switch to the ON position.
Check that no buzzer sounds when the vehicle speed is 20 km/h (12 mph) or more.

---

### POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > OPERATION CHECK

POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > OPERATION CHECK
for Preparation
Click here
CHECK OPENING OPERATION
Conditions necessary for the power slide door to open:
Auto slide door main switch is in the ON position.
Slide door is positioned between the fully closed position and the half-open position (When the slide door is fully closed, the door must be unlocked).
Shift lever is in the P position when the ignition switch is ON.
Press the auto slide door control switch for the power slide door.
Check that the power slide door opens when the switch has been pressed for more than 0.5 seconds.
HINT:
The power slide door can operate even when the child lock is ON.
Check that the power slide door reverses operation and moves to the fully closed position when the same switch is pressed again during opening.
Pull the outside / inside handle for the power slide door.
Check that the power slide door opens when the handle is pulled.
HINT:
For the inside handle, the power slide door can operate only when the child lock is OFF.
For the outside handle, the power slide door can operate even when the child lock is ON.
Check that the power slide door reverses operation and moves to the fully closed position when the same handle is pulled again during opening.
CHECK CLOSING OPERATION
Conditions necessary for the power slide door to close:
Auto slide door main switch is in the ON position.
Slide door is positioned between the fully open position and the half-open position.
Press the auto slide door control switch for the power slide door.
Check that the power slide door closes when the switch has been pressed for more than 0.5 seconds.
HINT:
The power slide door can operate even when the child lock is ON.
Check that the power slide door reverses operation and moves to the fully open position when the same switch is pressed again during closing.
Pull the outside / inside handle for the power slide door.
Check that the power slide door closes when the handle is pulled.
Check that the power slide door reverses operation and moves to the fully open position when the same handle is pulled again during closing.
CHECK JAM PROTECTION FUNCTION
CAUTION:
Do not use any part of the body to check this function. Make sure that nothing becomes accidentally caught in the power slide door during operation.
HINT:
The power slide door motor and power slide door clutch detect changes in the operating speed of the slide door while the power slide door is moving. If a change in the operating speed is out of the normal range, it will be judged to be a jam or a foreign object detected in the direction that the power slide door is moving.
When the power slide door sensor built into the front end of the slide door is compressed, it is judged to be a jam or a foreign object detected in the direction that the power slide door is moving, and the power slide door movement will reverse.
The power slide door sensor operates only while the slide door is closing. It does not operate while the slide door is opening.
Check the jam protection function while the power slide door is closing.
Fully open the slide door.
Press the switch that operates the power slide door to close it.
While the slide door is closing, hold the slide door by hand to decrease the door closing speed.
Check that the buzzer sounds for 0.1 seconds and the slide door reverses operation to the fully open position.
HINT:
Perform this check when the slide door closes 30 cm or more from the fully open position.
Check the jam protection function while the power slide door is opening.
Fully close the slide door.
Press the switch that operates the power slide door to open it.
When the slide door reaches the position where the distance to the fully open position is 20 cm or less, hold the slide door by hand to decrease the door opening speed
Check that the slide door stops moving.
Repeat steps (1) and (2).
Before the slide door reaches the position where the distance to the fully open position is approximately 20 cm, hold the slide door by hand to decrease the door opening speed.
Check that the slide door reverses operation to the fully closed position.
Check the operation of the power slide door when the jam protection function is consecutively activated.
Check that the power slide door operation is cancelled and switched to the manual operation mode (not electrically controlled) when the jam protection function has been activated 2 or more consecutive times and when the 2nd or most recent activation has occurred during closing.
HINT:
Refer to the previous 2 steps for methods to activate the jam protection function.
NOTICE:
To restore the power slide door system to the normal operation mode, perform one of the following:
Manually close the slide door fully.
Press the auto slide door control switch again.

### POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > DIAGNOSIS SYSTEM

POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > DIAGNOSIS SYSTEM
for Preparation
Click here
SELF-DIAGNOSTIC SYSTEM
HINT:
The self-diagnostic system has 3 modes: operation confirmation mode, input confirmation mode and malfunctioning operation memory mode.
In operation confirmation mode, the system can be checked for switch malfunctions by performing a predetermined door operation. If a switch malfunction is detected, a code will be output and a buzzer will sound.
In input confirmation mode, the system checks if each switch is inputting data properly. If a switch malfunction is detected, a buzzer will sound.
In malfunctioning operation memory mode, switch malfunctions that are detected while using the system are saved in memory. Then the memory can be accessed and read.
3 modes are available for use with the self-diagnostic system inspection: self-diagnostic mode 1, self-diagnostic mode 2 and self-diagnostic mode 3.
During self-diagnostic mode 1, the operation confirmation mode's switch malfunction detection function and input confirmation mode are performed.
During self-diagnostic mode 2, the switch malfunctions that were detected during operation confirmation mode (self-diagnostic mode 1) are output by sounding buzzer patterns.
During self-diagnostic mode 3, malfunctioning operation memory mode is entered and codes are output by sounding buzzer patterns.

---

### POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > DTC CHECK / CLEAR

POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > DTC CHECK / CLEAR
for Preparation
Click here
1.VEHICLE INITIAL SETTING
Stop the engine.
Prepare the vehicle by making sure these slide operational conditions are met.
Battery voltage is 11 V or more.
The auto slide door main switch is ON.
The slide door lock is unlocked (the position switch is ON).
The shift lever is in the P position.
Fully close the slide door.
NEXT
2.SELF-DIAGNOSTIC MODE 1
Enter self-diagnostic mode 1.
Turn OFF the auto slide door main switch.
Turn the ignition switch ON.
Within 10 seconds, push the auto slide door control switch 10 times.
Check that the buzzer sounds (see buzzer pattern 1).
Within 3 seconds, turn ON the auto slide door main switch.
Check that the buzzer sounds (see buzzer pattern 2) and that the system enters self-diagnostic mode 1.
Start the inspection.
NOTICE:
If the slide door operational conditions are not followed exactly, the system will change from self-diagnostic mode to normal operation mode.
Push the auto slide door control switch and fully open the slide door.
Pull the inside door handle, and fully close the slide door.
NOTICE:
If the outside door handle is operated during this inspection, a self-diagnostic code may be output when the system enters self-diagnostic mode 2, regardless of the status (normal/malfunctioning) of the slide door.
Perform the operations described in the table below.
When changes in the input signals are detected, check that the buzzer emits a sound for 0.5 seconds. If the buzzer does not sound, proceed to the relevant troubleshooting page in accordance with the table.
Target Items
Inspection Operations
Proceed to
Shift lever (Park/Neutral Position switch)
P position ←→ Expect P
(Release shift lock before inspection)
A
Vehicle speed
Less than 3 km/h (1 mph) ←→ 3 km/h (1 mph) or more
B
Power slide door lock assembly LH (door lock position switch)
ON ←→ OFF
C
Power slide door sensor
ON ←→ OFF
D
HINT:
When necessary to return to the normal operation mode, go to step 5.

3.SELF-DIAGNOSTIC MODE 2
Change from self-diagnostic mode 1 to self-diagnostic mode 2.
After the system has changed to self-diagnostic mode 1, press the auto slide door control switch for less than 0.5 seconds.
If the buzzer sounds twice, the system has successfully entered self-diagnostic mode 2.
Start the inspection.
Check that codes that have been detected during self-diagnostic mode 1 are output using these buzzer patterns.
Next step
Proceed to
Normal operation mode
A
Self-diagnostic mode 3

NOTICE:
If the system returns to normal operation mode from self-diagnostic mode, the codes saved in its memory will be erased. When necessary, change to self-diagnostic mode 3 and perform confirmation before returning to normal operation mode.
A
.
Go to step 6
B
4.SELF-DIAGNOSTIC MODE 3
Change from self-diagnostic mode 2 to self-diagnostic mode 3.
After the system has changed to self-diagnostic mode 2, press the auto slide door control switch for less than 0.5 seconds.
If the buzzer sounds twice, the system has successfully entered self-diagnostic mode 3.
Start the inspection.
Check the codes that were recorded in memory when a malfunction was detected in past operations through buzzer patterns.
HINT:
Codes are output in the same way as in self-diagnostic mode 2.
Change from self-diagnostic mode 3 to normal operation mode.
NOTICE:
If the system returns to normal operation mode from self-diagnostic mode, all the codes saved in its memory will be erased.
HINT:
When one of the following conditions is met, the buzzer sounds twice and changes to normal operation mode.
The ignition switch is turned OFF.
The auto slide door main switch is turned OFF.
The auto slide door control switch is pushed for less than 0.5 seconds.
END
.
TO NORMAL OPERATION MODE
5.CHANGE FROM SELF-DIAGNOSTIC MODE 1 TO NORMAL OPERATION MODE
The system changes from self-diagnostic mode 1 to normal operation mode when either of the following conditions is met:
The ignition switch is turned OFF.
The auto slide door main switch is turned OFF.
END
.
TO NORMAL OPERATION MODE
6.CHANGE FROM SELF-DIAGNOSTIC MODE 2 TO NORMAL OPERATION MODE
The system changes from self-diagnostic mode 2 to normal operation mode when either of the following conditions is met:
NOTICE:
If the system returns to normal operation mode from self-diagnostic mode, all the codes saved in its memory will be erased.
The ignition switch is turned OFF.
The auto slide door main switch is turned OFF.
END
TO NORMAL OPERATION MODE

for Preparation
Click here
DESCRIPTION
The ultrasonic sensor sends and receives ultrasonic waves. Based on the received wave, the sensor calculates the approximate distance between the vehicle and the obstacle, and sends the distance value as a signal to the clearance warning ECU.

INSPECTION PROCEDURE
1.INSPECT NO. 1 ULTRASONIC SENSOR (FRONT CORNER SENSOR LH)
Remove the No. 1 ultrasonic sensor (front corner sensor LH) (
Click here
).
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard Resistance:
Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Condition
1 (E) - 2 (S)
Always
20 to 40 kΩ
NG
REPLACE NO. 1 ULTRASONIC SENSOR (FRONT CORNER SENSOR LH) (
Click here
)
OK
2.CHECK HARNESS AND CONNECTOR (FRONT CORNER SENSOR LH - CLEARANCE WARNING ECU)
Disconnect the C1 No. 1 ultrasonic sensor (front corner sensor LH) connector.
Disconnect the H54 clearance warning ECU connector.
Measure the resistance according to the value(s) in the table below.
Standard Resistance:
Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Condition
C1-2 (S) - H54-13 (S6)
Always
Below 1 Ω
C1-1 (E) - H54-12 (E6)
Always
Below 1 Ω
C1-2 (S) - Body ground
Always
10 kΩ or higher
C1-1 (E) - Body ground
Always
10 kΩ or higher
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR
OK

Click here
)

### POWER SLIDE DOOR MAIN SWITCH > INSPECTION

POWER SLIDE DOOR MAIN SWITCH > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT POWER SLIDE DOOR MAIN SWITCH
Check the resistance.
Using an ohmmeter, measure the resistance and check the results in accordance with the value(s) in the table below.
Standard resistance:
Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Condition
3 - 4
Switch released
10 kΩ or higher
3 - 4
Switch pressed in
Below 1 Ω
4 - 5
Switch released
Below 1 Ω
4 - 5
Switch pressed in
10 kΩ or higher
If the result is not as specified, replace the power slide door main switch.
Check the illumination operation.
Apply battery voltage to the power slide door main switch and check that the illumination comes on.
Standard:
Measurement Condition
Specified Condition
Battery positive (+) → Terminal 2
Battery negative (-) → Terminal 1
Illumination comes on
If the result is not as specified, replace the power slide door main switch.

### DIESEL THROTTLE BODY > INSTALLATION

DIESEL THROTTLE BODY > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL DIESEL THROTTLE BODY ASSEMBLY
Install a new gasket onto the intake air connector.
Install the throttle body with the 2 bolts and 2 nuts.
Torque:
20 N*m{
204 kgf*cm
, 15 ft.*lbf
}
Connect the 2 throttle body connectors.
2. INSTALL NO. 4 AIR HOSE
Install the No. 4 air hose with the 2 clamps.
Torque:
6.0 N*m{
61 kgf*cm
, 53 in.*lbf
}
3. INSTALL VANE PUMP OIL RESERVOIR ASSEMBLY
Install the vane pump oil reservoir assembly with the 2 bolts.
Torque:
8.0 N*m{
82 kgf*cm
, 71 in.*lbf
}
4. CONNECT CABLE TO NEGATIVE BATTERY TERMINAL
Torque:
5.4 N*m{
55 kgf*cm
, 48 in.*lbf
}
5. PERFORM INITIALIZATION
Perform initialization procedures (
Click here
).
NOTICE:
Certain systems need to be initialized after reconnecting the cable to the negative (-) battery terminal.

### DIESEL THROTTLE BODY > REMOVAL

DIESEL THROTTLE BODY > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. DISCONNECT CABLE FROM NEGATIVE BATTERY TERMINAL
2. SEPARATE VANE PUMP OIL RESERVOIR ASSEMBLY
Remove the 2 bolts, and separate the vane pump oil reservoir assembly.
3. REMOVE NO. 4 AIR HOSE
Loosen the 2 clamps.
Remove the No. 4 air hose.
4. REMOVE DIESEL THROTTLE BODY ASSEMBLY
Disconnect the 2 throttle body connectors.
Remove the 2 bolts, 2 nuts, and the diesel throttle body assembly.
Remove the gasket from the intake air connector.

Remove the No. 4 air hose.
3. REMOVE DIESEL THROTTLE BODY ASSEMBLY
Disconnect the diesel turbo pressure sensor connector and vacuum switching valve connector.
Remove the bolt and disconnect the wire harness clamp.
Disconnect the 2 diesel throttle body connectors.
Remove the 2 bolts, 2 nuts, diesel throttle body and gasket.

### MASS AIR FLOW METER > REMOVAL

MASS AIR FLOW METER > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here

2. REMOVE RADIATOR GRILLE

3. REMOVE STEP PLATE COVER LH
4. REMOVE STEP PLATE COVER RH
5. REMOVE FRONT BUMPER

Release the 12 claws and remove the bumper.

Remove the 4 bolts, then remove the air cleaner assembly.
Disconnect the mass air flow meter connector.

Remove the 2 screws and the mass air flow meter.

### MASS AIR FLOW METER > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

MASS AIR FLOW METER > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
NOTICE:
Perform the mass air flow meter inspection according to the procedures below.
Only replace the mass air flow meter when the MAF value in the Data List (with the engine stopped) is not within the normal operating range or there is foreign matter on the MAF meter platinum hot wire (heater).
1. INSPECT INTAKE AIR FLOW METER
Read values using an intelligent tester (MAF).
NOTICE:
Turn the ignition switch OFF.
Perform the inspection with the vehicle indoors and on a level surface.
Perform the inspection of the mass air flow meter while it is installed in the air cleaner case (installed on the vehicle).
During the test, do not use the exhaust air duct to perform suction on the exhaust pipe.
Turn the ignition switch ON (do not run the engine).
Turn the tester ON.
Select the following menu items: Powertrain / Engine / Data List / MAF.
Wait 30 seconds, and read the values on the intelligent tester.
Standard condition:
Less than 0.38 g/sec.
If the result is not as specified, replace the mass air flow meter.
If the result is within the specified range, inspect the MAF meter (
Click here
).

---

### MASS AIR FLOW METER > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION

MASS AIR FLOW METER > ON-VEHICLE INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
NOTICE:
Perform the mass air flow meter inspection according to the procedures below.
Only replace the mass air flow meter when the mass air flow value in the Data List is not within the normal operating range (with the engine stopped) or there is foreign matter on the mass air flow meter platinum hot wire (heater).
1. INSPECT MASS AIR FLOW METER
Read the value using the intelligent tester (MAF).
NOTICE:
Perform the inspection with the vehicle indoors and on a level surface.
Perform the inspection of the mass air flow meter while it is installed to the air cleaner case (installed to the vehicle).
During the test, do not use the exhaust air duct to perform suction on the exhaust pipe.
Connect the intelligent tester to the DLC3.
Turn the ignition switch to ON (do not start the engine).
Turn the intelligent tester switch on.
Enter the following menus: Powertrain / Engine / Data List / MAF.
Wait 30 seconds, and then read the values on the intelligent tester.
Standard:
Less than 0.63 g/sec.
If the result is not as specified, replace the mass air flow meter.
If the result is within the specified range, inspect the mass air flow meter (
Click here
).

Measure the resistance.

Tester Connection
Condition
Specified Condition
q4-26 (SPD) - H20-9 (4S)
Always
Below 1 Ω
NG
REPAIR OR REPLACE HARNESS OR CONNECTOR

Click here
).
NG

### POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > Jam Protection Function Activates During Power Slide Door LH Operation

POWER SLIDE DOOR SYSTEM > Jam Protection Function Activates During Power Slide Door LH Operation
for Preparation
Click here
DESCRIPTION
The power slide door ECU LH controls the power slide door and activates the slide door motor. During motor operation, the power slide door touch sensor's resistance will change when it comes in contact with an object. When the change in resistance is detected, the power slide door ECU LH will determine that a jammed object is present.
The power slide door ECU LH also uses a pulse sensor to detect changes in the door's speed. If the door speed changes, the power slide door ECU LH assumes that a jammed object is present in the direction the power slide door is moving. When the power slide door is closing and a jammed object is detected, the door returns to the fully open position. When the power slide door is opening and a jammed object is detected, the door stops opening at the position where the jammed object was detected.
When the closer system is operating and a jammed object is detected, the closer system operation stops and the slide door will not fully close.
INSPECTION PROCEDURE
1.CHECK FOR FOREIGN OBJECTS
Check that no foreign objects are stuck between the vehicle body and the power slide door LH.
NG
REMOVE FOREIGN OBJECTS AND RE-CHECK SLIDE OPERATION
OK
2.CHECK MANUAL OPERATION
Turn OFF the power door switch. When the slide door is moved near the position where the jam is detected, check that the slide door moves smoothly by hand.
NG
CHECK FITTINGS OF SLIDE DOOR (
Click here
)
OK
REPLACE POWER SLIDE DOOR ECU LH

---

### POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR > INSTALLATION

POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSTALL POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR
Engage the 2 clips and install the power slide door touch sensor.
Tighten the 3 bolts.
Using "TORX" socket wrench T25, tighten the 6 screws.
Connect the connector.
2. INSTALL SLIDE DOOR SERVICE HOLE COVER LH
Apply butyl tape to the door panel, then install a new slide door service hole cover as shown in the illustration.
HINT:
When installing the service hole cover, pull the links and connectors out through the service hole.
There should be no wrinkles or folds after attaching the service hole cover.
After attaching the service hole cover, check the quality of the seal.
Connect the cable to the slide door lock remote control.
Engage the clamp.
3. INSTALL REAR NO. 2 DOOR WIRE
Engage the 4 clips and install the rear No. 2 door wire.
NOTICE:
Engage the clips by directly pushing them in by hand, not by tapping on the case surface.
Tighten the 4 bolts.
NOTICE:
Do not tighten the bolts with any wire harness or cable wedged under the case as it may cause the case to deform or malfunction.
Make sure that the wire harness is not twisted, and tighten the bolts after the clamp is fully secured by turning it in the direction indicated by the arrow mark.
Connect the connector.
4. INSTALL DOOR CONTROL RELAY ASSEMBLY
Install the door control relay with the bolt.
Connect the connectors.
5. INSTALL SIDE STEP REAR SUPPORT LH
Install the side step rear support with the 3 bolts.
Torque:
7.8 N*m{
80 kgf*cm
, 69 in.*lbf
}
6. INSTALL REAR DOOR SCUFF PLATE LH
Connect the connector.
Engage the claw and 12 clips and install the rear door scuff plate.
7. INSTALL REAR DOOR TRIM BOARD SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Engage the claw and 19 clips, and install the rear door trim board.
Using "TORX" socket wrench T25, tighten the 2 screws.
8. INSTALL SLIDE DOOR WINDOW GARNISH LH
Engage the 4 clips and install the slide door window garnish.
9. CONNECT CABLE TO NEGATIVE BATTERY TERMINAL
Torque:
5.4 N*m{
55 kgf*cm
, 48 in.*lbf
}

---

### POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR > INSPECTION

POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR > INSPECTION
for Preparation
Click here
1. INSPECT POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR
Check the resistance.
Using an ohmmeter, measure the resistance and check the results in accordance with the value(s) in the tables below.
Standard resistance:
Condition
Specified Condition
OFF(Sensor not pressed)
Approximately 1 kΩ
ON(Sensor pressed)
Approximately 100 Ω or less
If the result is not as specified, replace the power slide door touch sensor.

---

### POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR > REMOVAL

POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. DISCONNECT CABLE FROM NEGATIVE BATTERY TERMINAL
2. REMOVE SLIDE DOOR WINDOW GARNISH LH
Disengage the 4 clips and remove the slide door window garnish.
3. REMOVE REAR DOOR TRIM BOARD SUB-ASSEMBLY LH
Using "TORX" socket wrench T25, remove the 2 screws.
Disengage the claw and 19 clips, and remove the rear door trim board.
4. REMOVE REAR DOOR SCUFF PLATE LH
Disengage the claw and 12 clips.
Disconnect the connector and remove the rear door scuff plate.
5. REMOVE SIDE STEP REAR SUPPORT LH
Remove the 3 bolts and the side step rear support.
6. REMOVE DOOR CONTROL RELAY ASSEMBLY
Disconnect the connectors.
Remove the bolt and the door control relay.
7. REMOVE REAR NO. 2 DOOR WIRE
Remove the bolt.
Disconnect the connector from the vehicle.
Remove the 4 bolts, and then disengage the 4 clips.
Disconnect the connector of the door, and remove the rear No. 2 door wire.
NOTICE:
Do not put anything on the rear No. 2 door wire after removal.
8. REMOVE SLIDE DOOR SERVICE HOLE COVER LH
Disengage the clamp.
Disconnect the cable from the slide door lock remote control.
Remove the slide door service hole cover.
9. REMOVE POWER SLIDE DOOR TOUCH SENSOR
Disconnect the connector.
Using "TORX" socket wrench T25, remove the 6 screws.
Remove the 3 bolts.
Disengage the 2 clips and the power slide door touch sensor.

---

### TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION

TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM > SYSTEM DESCRIPTION
for Preparation
Click here
GENERAL
When the ultrasonic sensors detect any obstacles at the corners and rear of the vehicle, the system informs the driver of the distance between the sensors and the obstacles by sounding a buzzer.
FUNCTION OF COMPONENTS
Components
Function
Clearance warning ECU
Receives signals from the ultrasonic sensors. Based on the received wave, the ECU calculates the approximate distance between the vehicle and obstacle, and sends a buzzer signal to the clearance warning buzzer.
Front passenger seat belt warning light assembly (Clearance sonar main switch)
Supplies electrical power to the sensors and outputs system on/off signals to the clearance warning ECU.
Front passenger seat belt warning light assembly (Clearance warning buzzer)
Sounds to inform the driver of the distance to the obstacle.
Front passenger seat belt warning light assembly (Clearance warning indicator)
Illuminates the location of the obstacle and the approximate distance between the vehicle and the obstacle, and turns the TOYOTA parking assist-sensor system on and off.
No. 1 ultrasonic sensor
The ultrasonic sensor (front corner, rear corner) sends and receives ultrasonic waves.
No. 2 ultrasonic sensor
The ultrasonic sensor (rear center) sends and receives ultrasonic waves.
Park/neutral position switch assembly*1
Back-up light switch assembly*2
Sends a signal that activates the TOYOTA parking assist-sensor system when the shift lever is moved to R.
*1: for Automatic Transmission
*2: for Manual Transmission
OPERATION EXPLANATION
The clearance warning ECU determines whether the TOYOTA parking assist-sensor system should be operated based on the clearance sonar main switch on/off status and the shift position.
When the system operates, the ECU transmits ultrasonic waves from the ultrasonic sensors. If these waves encounter an obstacle within the range of one or more of the sensors, the waves are reflected back to the sensors, which transmit them to the ECU. Based on this information, the ECU sends signals to the clearance warning buzzer. The approximate distance between the vehicle and the obstacle is then indicated, and the buzzer sounds.
HINT:
Refer to "Operation Check" for the detailed operation (
Click here
).

---

### TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM > OPERATION CHECK

TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM > OPERATION CHECK
for Preparation
Click here
CHECK INITIAL CHECK FUNCTION
Check the initial check function for the buzzer.
When the clearance sonar main switch assembly is turned on, check that the following occurs: 1) after 0.1 seconds, the buzzer sounds for approximately 0.8 seconds, and 2) the system starts the obstacle detection operation.
Check the initial check function for the indicator.
When the clearance sonar main switch assembly is turned on, check that the following occurs: 1) after 0.1 seconds, the sensor LED illuminates for approximately 0.8 seconds, and 2) the system starts the obstacle detection operation.
After the clearance sonar main switch assembly is turned on, check that the vehicle LED illuminates.
Check the initial check function for the sensor.
Approximately 0.1 seconds after the ignition switch is turned to ON and the clearance sonar main switch is turned on, all the sensors are checked by the system.
When the shift lever is operated, the system starts the obstacle detection operation.
MALFUNCTION INDICATOR AND BUZZER
Open circuit indication
If there is an open circuit between the ultrasonic sensor and the clearance warning ECU, or a sensor is malfunctioning, the malfunction is displayed as shown in the illustration.
HINT:
The example shows an open circuit in the ultrasonic sensor (front left sensor).
Troubleshoot according to each inspection procedure (
Click here
).
Frozen indication
If a sensor is covered with foreign matter, such as mud or snow, the affected sensor is displayed as shown in the illustration.
HINT:
The example shows that the ultrasonic sensor (front left sensor) is covered with foreign matter.
Troubleshoot according to each inspection procedure (
Click here
).
DETECTION RANGE MEASUREMENT AND INDICATOR CHECK
CAUTION:
Apply the parking brake securely so that the vehicle does not move.
Turn the ignition switch to ON.
Turn the clearance sonar main switch on.
Move the shift lever to R to check the rear corner sensors and rear center sensors.
Move a pole around the sensor to measure the detection range of the sensor.
NOTICE:
These detection ranges are applicable when positioning a pole with a diameter of φ60 mm (2.36 in.) parallel or perpendicular to the ground.
The ranges vary depending on the measuring method and type of obstacle (such as walls).
For close-range and medium-range detection, the values shown are for when using a pole with a diameter of φ60 mm (2.36 in.). For long-range detection, the values shown are for when using walls.
The No. 1 ultrasonic sensor side view detection range hatched area*1 represents the cross section of the top view of the detection range along the lines labeled A. The hatched area*1 does not represent the entire side view detection range.

Rear corner sensor detection range
Rear center sensor detection range
When the ultrasonic sensors detect an obstacle with the clearance sonar main switch on, check that the buzzer sounds.

Corner indicator(s) are blinking and/or back indicator is blinking
4. Maximum long-range detection
Rear center indicator is blinking
HINT:
Ultrasonic waves are used to measure the detection range. However, the detection range may vary depending on the ambient temperature.

### TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM > PRECAUTION

TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM > PRECAUTION
for Preparation
Click here
1.PRECAUTION FOR TOYOTA PARKING ASSIST-SENSOR SYSTEM
HINT:
In very cold weather, the sensor may not detect obstacles.
If the malfunction display appears, visually check the sensor first. If the sensor is free of foreign matter but the malfunction display remains, the sensor may have a malfunction.
Under the following conditions, the detection function may not operate properly:
The sensor is covered with foreign matter, such as mud or snow (detection function returns to normal if sensor is cleaned).
The sensor is frozen (detection function returns to normal when defrosted).
A hand is blocking the sensor.
The detection range may be affected by the following conditions:
The sensor is covered with foreign matter, such as mud or snow.
The vehicle is in a very hot or very cold area.
Under the following conditions, a detection error may occur:
Driving on a bumpy or unpaved road, or in tall grass.
Things that transmit ultrasonic waves, such as other vehicle horns or sonar systems, motorcycle engines, or large vehicle air brakes, are near the vehicle.
It is raining heavily or water is sprayed on the sensor.
The vehicle is extremely tilted.
The vehicle is equipped with a commercial fender pole or a wireless antenna.
The sensor is covered with foreign matter, such as mud or snow.
The vehicle is moved forward or backward towards high curbs or objects that are perpendicular to the ground.
The sensor cannot detect the following objects.
Thin objects, such as wires and ropes.
Materials that easily absorb ultrasonic waves, such as cotton, snow, etc.
Objects with sharp edges.
Short objects.
Tall objects with a projected upper part.
Other notices:
The sensor cannot detect objects directly under the bumper (the sensor may detect low objects and thin poles, and then lose track of the objects).
The sensor may be unable to detect obstacles that are too close to the sensor.
The sensor may be unable to detect obstacles if it is dropped or subjected to a strong impact, especially on the membrane.

### ROOF WINDOW FIX GLASS > INSTALLATION

ROOF WINDOW FIX GLASS > INSTALLATION
for Preparation
Click here
1. CLEAN VEHICLE BODY
Clean and shape the contact surface of the vehicle body.
On the contact surface of the vehicle body, use a knife to cut away excess adhesive as shown in the illustration.
HINT:
Leave as much adhesive on the vehicle body as possible.
NOTICE:
Be careful not to damage the vehicle body.
Clean the contact surface of the vehicle body with cleaner.
HINT:
Even if all the adhesive has been removed, clean the vehicle body.
2. INSTALL ROOF WINDOW FIX GLASS
Using a brush or sponge, apply Primer M to the exposed part of the vehicle body.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer M to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Do not apply Primer M to the adhesive.
Throw away any leftover Primer M.
Do not apply too much Primer M.
Using a brush or sponge, apply Primer G to the contact surface of the roof window fix glass.
Specification:
Area
Measurement
a
14.8 mm (0.583 in.)
b
4.0 mm (0.157 in.)
c
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
HINT:
If Primer G is applied to an area other than that which is specified, wipe off the primer with non-residue solvent before it dries.
NOTICE:
Allow the Primer G to dry for 3 minutes or more.
Throw away any leftover Primer G.
Do not apply too much Primer G.
Apply adhesive to a new roof window fix glass.
Adhesive:
Toyota Genuine Windshield Glass Adhesive or equivalent
Cut off the tip of cartridge nozzle as shown in the illustration.
Specification:
Area
Measurement
a
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
b
12.0 mm (0.472 in.)
HINT:
After cutting off the tip, use all adhesive within the time written in the table below.
Usage time frame:
Temperature
Usage Time Frame
35°C (95°F)
15 minutes
20°C (68°F)
1 hour 40 minutes
5°C (41°F)
8 hours
Load the sealer gun with the cartridge.
Apply adhesive to the roof window fix glass as shown in the illustration.
Specification:
Area
Measurement
a
14.8 mm (0.583 in.)
b
8.0 mm (0.315 in.)
c
12 mm (0.472 in.)
Install the roof window fix glass to the vehicle body.
Lightly press the front surface of the roof window fix glass to ensure that the roof window fix glass is securely fit to the vehicle body.
NOTICE:
Check that the spacers are attached to the vehicle body correctly.
Check that the vehicle body and roof window fix glass have a small gap between them.
Hold the roof window fix glass in place securely with protective tape or equivalent until the adhesive hardens.
NOTICE:
Do not drive the vehicle for the amount of time written in the table below.
Minimum time:
Temperature
Minimum time prior to driving vehicle
35°C (95°F)
1 hour 30 minutes
20°C (68°F)
5 hours
5°C (41°F)
24 hours
3. CHECK FOR LEAKAGE AND REPAIR
After the adhesive has hardened, apply water from the outside of the vehicle. Check that no water leaks into the cabin.
If water leaks into the cabin, allow the water to dry and add adhesive.
Remove the protective tape.
4. INSTALL ROOF HEADLINING GARNISH
Engage the 16 clips and install the roof headlining garnish.

### ROOF WINDOW FIX GLASS > REMOVAL

ROOF WINDOW FIX GLASS > REMOVAL
for Preparation
Click here
1. REMOVE ROOF HEADLINING GARNISH
Using a moulding remover, disengage the 16 clips and remove the roof headlining garnish.
2. REMOVE ROOF WINDOW FIX GLASS
Apply protective tape to the outer surface of the vehicle body to prevent scratches.
From the interior, insert piano wires between the vehicle body and roof window fix glass as shown in the illustration.
Tie objects that can serve as handles (for example, wooden blocks) to all wire ends.
NOTICE:
When separating the roof window fix glass from the vehicle paint or interior/exterior ornaments.
Cut through the adhesive by pulling the piano wire around the roof window fix glass.
Using a suction cup, disengage the 4 clips and remove the roof window fix glass.
NOTICE:
Leave as much adhesive on the vehicle body as possible when removing the roof window fix glass.
